# September 11 attacks

This article is about the 2001 attacks in the United States. For other events, see [September 11 attacks (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11_attacks_\(disambiguation\)).

"9/11" and "Nine Eleven" redirect here. For the calendar dates, see [September 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11) and [November 9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_9). For other uses, see [911](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/911_\(disambiguation\)).

The **September 11 attacks**,[^cite_note-12] colloquially known as **9/11**,[^cite_note-14] were a coordinated series of [suicide attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_attacks) perpetrated by the [Islamic terrorist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_terrorist) organization [al-Qaeda](1921.md) against the United States in 2001. A total of [19 hijackers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hijackers_in_the_September_11_attacks) commandeered four commercial passenger [airliners](51215.md). The first two planes were flown into each of the [Twin Towers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twin_Towers) at the [World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\)) in [New York City](645042.md), while the third plane crashed into [the Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon), the headquarters of the [United States Department of Defense](7279897.md), in [Arlington County, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia). The fourth plane crashed in a rural field in [Pennsylvania](23332.md) during a passenger revolt.

The ringleader, [Mohamed Atta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Atta), flew [American Airlines Flight 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines_Flight_11) into the [North Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1_World_Trade_Center_\(1970%E2%80%932001\)) of the World Trade Center at 8\:46 a.m. At 9\:03 a.m.[^cite_note-Time-20] [Marwan al-Shehhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marwan_al-Shehhi) flew [United Airlines Flight 175](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines_Flight_175) into the [South Tower](19372810.md). [Both collapsed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collapse_of_the_World_Trade_Center) within an hour and forty-two minutes,[^cite_note-Time1-25] destroying the remaining five structures in the complex. [Hani Hanjour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hani_Hanjour) flew [American Airlines Flight 77](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines_Flight_77) into the Pentagon at 9\:37 a.m., causing a partial collapse. [United Airlines Flight 93](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines_Flight_93), hijacked by [Ziad Jarrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ziad_Jarrah), was believed to target either the [United States Capitol](31979.md) or the [White House](33057.md). Alerted to the previous attacks, the passengers revolted and the hijackers crashed the aircraft into a field near [Shanksville, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanksville,_Pennsylvania), at 10\:03 a.m. The [Federal Aviation Administration](11186.md) ordered a [ground stop](19108133.md) for all traffic in U.S. [airspace](353557.md), requiring all airborne aircraft to return to their point of origin or divert to Canada. The actions undertaken in Canada to support incoming aircraft and their occupants were collectively titled [Operation Yellow Ribbon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Yellow_Ribbon).

That evening, the [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) informed President [George W. Bush](3414021.md) that its [Counterterrorism Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterterrorism_Center) had identified the attacks as having been the work of al-Qaeda under [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden). [The United States responded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._government_response_to_the_September_11_attacks) by [invading Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Afghanistan) and launching its [Global War on Terror](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_War_on_Terror), with the stated goal of eliminating hostile groups it deemed [terrorist organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorist_organizations) and the governments the United States identified as supporting them. [NATO](21133.md)'s invocation of [Article 5 of the North Atlantic Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_5_contingency_\(2001\))—its only usage to date—called upon allies to fight al-Qaeda as well. As U.S. and allied invasion forces swept through Afghanistan, bin Laden eluded them. He denied any involvement until 2004, when excerpts of [a taped statement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Osama_bin_Laden_video) in which he accepted [responsibility for the attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Responsibility_for_the_September_11_attacks) were released. [Al-Qaeda's cited motivations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motives_for_the_September_11_attacks) included [U.S. support of Israel](3449549.md), the presence of [U.S. military bases in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Southern_Watch), and [sanctions against Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctions_against_Iraq). The nearly decade-long [manhunt for bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhunt_for_Osama_bin_Laden) concluded in May 2011, when [he was killed during a U.S. military raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Osama_bin_Laden) in [Abbottabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbottabad), Pakistan. The [war in Afghanistan](19666611.md) continued for another decade.

The attacks [killed 2,977 people, injured thousands more](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_September_11_attacks),[^cite_note-26] and gave rise to [long-term health consequences](7770119.md) while causing at least US\$10 billion in infrastructure and property damage. It remains the deadliest terrorist attack in history, as well as the [deadliest incident for firefighters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_deadliest_firefighter_disasters_in_the_United_States) and [law enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_law_enforcement_officers_killed_in_the_line_of_duty_in_the_United_States) personnel in American history, killing 343 and 72 members, respectively. The crashes of Flight 11 and Flight 175 were [the deadliest aviation disasters of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_deadliest_aircraft_accidents_and_incidents#Table), and the collision of Flight 77 with the Pentagon resulted in the fourth-highest number of ground fatalities in a plane crash in history. The destruction of the World Trade Center and [its environs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_buildings_damaged_or_destroyed_in_the_September_11_attacks) seriously harmed the U.S. economy and [induced global market shocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_effects_of_the_September_11_attacks). Many other countries [tightened airport security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airport_security_repercussions_due_to_the_September_11_attacks), strengthened [anti-terrorism legislation](4700071.md) and expanded their powers of [law enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_enforcement_agency) and [intelligence agencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intelligence_agencies). The total number of deaths caused by the attacks, combined with the death tolls from the conflicts they directly triggered, has been estimated by the [Costs of War Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costs_of_War_Project) to be more than 4.5 million.[^cite_note-\:2-27]

Cleanup of the [World Trade Center site](67173.md) (colloquially known as "Ground Zero") was completed in May 2002, while the Pentagon was repaired within a year. After delays in the design of a replacement complex, [six new buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(2001%E2%80%93present\)) were planned to replace the lost towers at the World Trade Center site, along with [a museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Museum) and memorial dedicated to those who were killed or injured in the attacks. The tallest building, [One World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_World_Trade_Center), began construction in 2006 and opened in 2014. [Memorials to the attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorials_and_services_for_the_September_11_attacks) include the [National September 11 Memorial & Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_September_11_Memorial_&_Museum) in New York City; the [Pentagon Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentagon_Memorial) in Arlington County, Virginia; and the [Flight 93 National Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flight_93_National_Memorial) at the Pennsylvania crash site.

## Background

### Osama bin Laden

Main article: [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden)

In 1996, Osama bin Laden, leading the [jihadist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jihadist) organization [al-Qaeda](1921.md), issued his first [fatwa](11259.md), which declared [jihad](16203.md) against the United States and demanded expulsion of all [American troops stationed in Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Southern_Watch).[^cite_note-\:3-28] Bin Laden interpreted [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad) as banning [non-Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafir) from Arabia.[^cite_note-29] He thus considered the U.S. troop presence a provocation to all Muslims.[^cite_note-30] Regarding his holy war, bin Laden stated, "We do not differentiate between those dressed in military uniforms, and civilians; they are all targets of this fatwa."[^cite_note-\:3-28]

[Bin Laden was living in Sudan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden's_house_in_Khartoum) prior to 1996. When the Sudanese government exiled him after Saudi and U.S. pressure,[^cite_note-\:03-31] bin Laden returned to Afghanistan, which was run by the [Taliban](30635.md). They allowed al-Qaeda to use the country as its base of operations.[^cite_note-\:03-31][^cite_note-32] He then orchestrated the 9/11 attacks on the U.S. He personally told his participants to target the [World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\)) and [the Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) with hijacked planes.[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34] He initially denied his role in the attacks, but later recanted his denial.[^cite_note-CBC_2004-10-29-35]

U.S. forces recovered a videotape in which bin Laden, talking to [Khaled al-Harbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaled_al-Harbi), [admitted foreknowledge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Videos_and_audio_recordings_of_Osama_bin_Laden#December_13,_2001) of the attacks.[^cite_note-36] In [a 2004 video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Osama_bin_Laden_video), he confirmed that he had organized 9/11.[^cite_note-cbc-2004-37][^cite_note-38] A video by Al Jazeera in 2006 shows bin Laden with one of the attacks' chief planners, [Ramzi bin al-Shibh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramzi_bin_al-Shibh), as well as hijackers [Hamza al-Ghamdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamza_al-Ghamdi) and [Wail al-Shehri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wail_al-Shehri).[^cite_note-39]

#### Motives

Main article: [Motives for the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motives_for_the_September_11_attacks)

Further information: [Fatwas of Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatwas_of_Osama_bin_Laden) and [Political views of Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_views_of_Osama_bin_Laden)



[Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden)

Bin Laden's 1996 fatwa, in addition to similar statements that called for the killing of Americans, are seen by investigators as evidence of his motivation for the attacks.[^cite_note-40][^cite_note-PBS_2013-11-28-41][^cite_note-1996_text-42] In a second fatwa in 1998, he outlined more of his objections to [American foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_foreign_policy), such as [American support of Israel](3449549.md)[^cite_note-PBS_2013-11-28-41] and the U.S. and other nations' [sanctions against Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctions_against_Iraq), condemning the "protracted blockade."[^cite_note-1998_Al_Qaeda_fatwa-43] He claimed that the U.S. was being directed by an [international Jewish conspiracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Jewish_conspiracy) into killing as many Muslims as possible,[^cite_note-May1998-44][^cite_note-Time1999-45] and that all Muslims must wage a defensive war against the U.S.[^cite_note-\:4-46] This was to be done until the aggression against them ceased.[^cite_note-PBS_2013-11-28-41][^cite_note-47] Bin Laden further claimed it would send a message to the American people, forcing the U.S. to reevaluate its policies.[^cite_note-\:26-48] In a 1998 interview with American journalist [John Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Miller_\(police_official\)), he stated:[^cite_note-49]

> American history does not distinguish between civilians and military, not even women and children. They are the ones who used \[a [nuclear bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_bomb)] [against Nagasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_bombings_of_Hiroshima_and_Nagasaki). Can these bombs distinguish between infants and military? America does not have a religion that will prevent it from destroying all people. So we tell the Americans as people \[...] that if they value their lives and the lives of their children, to find a [nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist) government that will look after their interests and not the interests of the Jews.

After 9/11, bin Laden maintained that women and children were not targeted in the attack—rather, symbols of America's "economic and military power."[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51] In December 2001, a video of bin Laden was released in which he did not admit responsibility for 9/11, but instead said:[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-\:10-53]

> It has become clear that the West in general and America in particular have an unspeakable hatred for Islam. \[...] It is the hatred of [crusaders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crusaders). Terrorism against America deserves to be praised because it was a response to injustice, aimed at forcing America to stop its support for Israel, which kills our people. \[...] We say that the end of the United States is imminent, whether bin Laden or his followers are alive or dead, for the awakening of the Muslim *[ummah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ummah)* \[nation] has occurred. \[...] It is important to hit the economy \[of the U.S.], which is the base of its military power... If the economy is hit, they will become reoccupied.

In [a 2002 manifesto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Letter_to_the_American_People), he listed multiple factors implied to have motivated 9/11, including U.S. support of Israel against Lebanon during their [occupation of Southern Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_Southern_Lebanon_\(1982%E2%80%932000\)) and Palestinians during the [Second Intifada](71717.md), support of the Philippines [against Muslim militants](4602307.md), support of Russia [against Muslim militants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Chechen_War), and support of India [against Muslim civilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violence_against_Muslims_in_India) in [Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashmir). He also listed the former [U.S.-led intervention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unified_Task_Force) against Muslim militants in Somalia, [pollution caused by the U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pollution_in_the_United_States), and the U.S.' [refusal to ratify the Kyoto Protocol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyoto_Protocol#Non-ratification_by_the_US).[^cite_note-54] In the 2004 video, he said he was inspired to destroy the World Trade Center's [Twin Towers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\)) after watching the [destruction of towers in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Beirut) by Israel during the [1982 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-cbc-2004-37][^cite_note-55]

> "God knows it did not cross our minds to attack the towers, but after \[witnessing] \[...] the destroyed towers in Lebanon, it occurred to me \[to] punish the unjust the same way: to destroy towers in America, so it could taste some of what we are tasting, and to stop killing our children and women."[^cite_note-Guardian-56]

Other motives have been suggested in addition to those stated by him and other al-Qaeda members. Some authors suggested the "humiliation" that resulted from the Islamic world falling behind the Western world—this discrepancy was rendered especially visible by globalization[^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58]—as well as a desire to provoke the U.S. into a broader war against the Islamic world in the hope of motivating more allies to support al-Qaeda. Similarly, others have argued the 9/11 attacks were a strategic move to provoke America into a war that would incite a [pan-Islamic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Islamic) revolution.[^cite_note-59][^cite_note-60]

### Khalid Sheikh Mohammed and other al-Qaeda members

Main article: [Khalid Sheikh Mohammed](190884.md)



[Khalid Sheikh Mohammed](190884.md) after his 2003 capture in [Rawalpindi](343524.md), Pakistan

In April 2002, [Yosri Fouda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yosri_Fouda) of Al Jazeera met al-Qaeda member [Khalid Sheikh Mohammed](190884.md) and bin al-Shibh, who were in hiding, and they admitted to him their involvement in the attacks.[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62][^cite_note-aljazeera2007-63] Mohammed had been an organizer and financier of al-Qaeda's [1993 bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_World_Trade_Center_bombing) of the World Trade Center, and he was the uncle of [Ramzi Yousef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramzi_Yousef), the lead bomber in that attack.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65] Mohammed and Yousef planned another terrorist attack, the [Bojinka plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bojinka_plot), for January 1995. Despite its failure, and [Yousef's imprisonment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramzi_Yousef#Arrest,_conviction_and_prison_life) by the U.S. afterwards, the plot would influence the later September 11 attacks.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006235–236-66] The 2004 *[9/11 Commission Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Commission_Report)* determined that the animosity that Mohammed, the principal architect of 9/11, felt towards the U.S. had stemmed from his "violent disagreement with U.S. foreign policy favoring Israel."[^cite_note-9/11_Commission_Report_2004_p147-67]

### Planning

Main article: [Planning of the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planning_of_the_September_11_attacks)

Documents seized during the [2011 operation that killed bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Osama_bin_Laden) included notes handwritten by bin Laden in September 2002 with the heading "The Birth of the Idea of September 11." He describes how he was inspired by the crash of [EgyptAir Flight 990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EgyptAir_Flight_990) in October 1999, which was deliberately crashed by co-pilot [Gameel Al-Batouti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gameel_Al-Batouti), killing more than 200 passengers. "This is how the idea of 9/11 was conceived and developed in my head, and that is when we began the planning" bin Laden continued, adding that no one but Mohammed Atef and Abu al-Khair knew about it at the time. The *9/11 Commission Report* identified Khalid Sheikh Mohammed as the architect of 9/11, but he is not mentioned in bin Laden's notes.[^cite_note-68]

The twin towers of the original World Trade Center, as seen in March 2001, before the September 11 attacks

The twin towers of the original [World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\)) complex as seen in March 2001, before the September 11 attacks

During the trial of alleged 9/11 conspirator [Zacarias Moussaoui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zacarias_Moussaoui), the U.S. government identified five people as having been completely aware of the operation's details; bin Laden, Mohammed, Mohammed Atef, [Abu Turab al-Urduni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Turab_al-Urduni), and bin al-Shibh.[^cite_note-69] The attacks were conceived by Khalid Sheikh Mohammed, who first presented it to bin Laden in 1996.[^cite_note-70] Many targets were listed that al-Qaeda hijackers could crash planes into, including the [Library Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Library_Tower) (now the U.S. Bank Tower) in Los Angeles.[^cite_note-71][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006308-72] Bin Laden rejected the plan for being too elaborate.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006-73][^cite_note-\:12-74] Al-Qaeda's first attacks against the U.S. after Bin Laden's 1998 fatwa were the [1998 African embassy bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_United_States_embassy_bombings).[^cite_note-75] In late 1998 or early 1999, bin Laden approved Mohammed to go forward with a new version of the 1996 plan.[^cite_note-76][^cite_note-\:03-31]

Bin Laden provided leadership and financial support, and was involved in selecting participants.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBergen2006283-77] Atef provided operational support, including target selections and helping arrange travel for the hijackers.[^cite_note-911-ch5-78] He initially selected [Nawaf al-Hazmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawaf_al-Hazmi) and [Khalid al-Mihdhar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalid_al-Mihdhar), both experienced jihadists who had fought in the [Bosnian war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_war). The two arrived in the United States in mid-January 2000. In early 2000, they took flying lessons in San Diego, California. Both spoke little English. They performed poorly in flying lessons, and so they eventually served as secondary "muscle" hijackers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006309–315-79][^cite_note-80]

The [Hamburg cell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamburg_cell) in Germany included Islamists who were key operatives in the 9/11 attacks.[^cite_note-81] In late 1999, cell members bin al-Shibh, [Mohamed Atta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Atta), [Marwan al-Shehhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marwan_al-Shehhi), and [Ziad Jarrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ziad_Jarrah) arrived to meet al-Qaeda in Afghanistan.[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83] Bin Laden selected these men because they were educated, could speak English, and had experience living in the West.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006304–307-84] New recruits were routinely screened for special skills and al-Qaeda leaders consequently discovered that [Hani Hanjour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hani_Hanjour) already had a commercial pilot's license.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006302-85]

Hanjour arrived in San Diego on December 8, 2000, joining al-Hazmi.[^cite_note-9/11_commission_staff_statement_No._16-86]<sup>: 6–7 </sup> They soon left for Arizona, where Hanjour took refresher training.[^cite_note-9/11_commission_staff_statement_No._16-86]<sup>: 7 </sup> Marwan al-Shehhi arrived at the end of May 2000, while Atta arrived on June 3, 2000, and Jarrah arrived on June 27, 2000.[^cite_note-9/11_commission_staff_statement_No._16-86]<sup>: 6 </sup> Bin al-Shibh applied several times for a visa to the United States, but as a Yemeni, he was rejected out of concerns he would overstay his visa.[^cite_note-9/11_commission_staff_statement_No._16-86]<sup>: 4, 14 </sup> Bin al-Shibh stayed in Hamburg, providing coordination between Atta and Mohammed.[^cite_note-9/11_commission_staff_statement_No._16-86]<sup>: 16 </sup> The three Hamburg cell members all took pilot training in South Florida at [Huffman Aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huffman_Aviation).[^cite_note-9/11_commission_staff_statement_No._16-86]<sup>: 6 </sup>

In the spring of 2001, the secondary hijackers began arriving in the United States.[^cite_note-87] In July 2001, Atta met with bin al-Shibh in Spain, where they coordinated details of the plot, including final target selection. Bin al-Shibh passed along bin Laden's wish for the attacks to be carried out as soon as possible.[^cite_note-irujo-88] Some of the hijackers received passports from corrupt Saudi officials who were family members or used fraudulent passports to gain entry.[^cite_note-89]

### Prior intelligence

Main article: [September 11 intelligence before the attacks](35176335.md)

In late 1999, al-Qaeda associate [Walid bin Attash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walid_bin_Attash) ("Khallad") contacted al-Mihdhar and told him to meet in [Kuala Lumpur](16854.md), Malaysia; al-Hazmi and [Abu Bara al Yemeni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Bara_al_Yemeni) would also be in attendance. The [NSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSA) intercepted a telephone call mentioning the meeting, al-Mihdhar, and the name "Nawaf" (al-Hazmi); while the agency feared "Something nefarious might be afoot", it took no further action.[^cite_note-The_Al_Qaeda_Switchboard-90]

The CIA had already been alerted by Saudi intelligence about al-Mihdhar and al-Hazmi being al-Qaeda members. A CIA team broke into al-Mihdhar's [Dubai](211583.md) hotel room and discovered that Mihdhar had a U.S. visa. While [Alec Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alec_Station) alerted intelligence agencies worldwide, it did not share this information with the FBI. The [Malaysian Special Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysian_Special_Branch) observed the January 5, 2000, meeting of the two al-Qaeda members and informed the CIA that al-Mihdhar, al-Hazmi, and Khallad were flying to [Bangkok](56615.md), but the CIA never notified other agencies of this, nor did it ask the [State Department](31975.md) to put al-Mihdhar on its watchlist. An FBI liaison asked permission to inform the FBI of the meeting but was told: "This is not a matter for the FBI."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006310–312-91]

&#x20;By late June, senior counter-terrorism official [Richard Clarke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_A._Clarke) and CIA director [George Tenet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Tenet) were "convinced that a major series of attacks was about to come", although the CIA believed the attacks would likely occur in Saudi Arabia or Israel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarke2004235–236-92] In early July, Clarke put domestic agencies on "full alert", telling them, "Something spectacular is going to happen here, and it's going to happen soon." He asked the FBI and the State Department to alert the embassies and police departments, and the [Defense Department](7279897.md) to go to "Threat Condition Delta."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006344-93][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarke2004236–237-94] Clarke later wrote:&#x20;

> Somewhere in CIA there was information that two known al-Qaeda terrorists had come into the United States. Somewhere in the FBI, there was information that strange things had been going on at flight schools in the United States. \[...] They had specific information about individual terrorists from which one could have deduced what was about to happen. None of that information got to me or the White House.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarke2004242–243-95]

> \[...] by July \[2001], with word spreading of a coming attack, a schism emerged among the senior leadership of al Qaeda. Several senior members reportedly agreed with [Mullah Omar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mullah_Omar). Those who reportedly sided with [bin Ladin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bin_Ladin) included [Atef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Atef), [Sulayman Abu Ghayth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulayman_Abu_Ghayth), and Khalid Sheikh Mohammed. But those said to have opposed him were weighty figures in the organization-including [Abu Hafs the Mauritanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahfouz_Ould_al-Walid), [Sheikh Saeed al Masri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saeed_al-Masri), and [Sayf al Adl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayf_al_Adl). One senior al Qaeda operative claims to recall Bin Ladin arguing that attacks against the United States needed to be carried out immediately to support [insurgency in the Israeli-occupied territories](71717.md) and protest the presence of U.S. forces in Saudi Arabia.

—  *[9/11 Commission Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Commission_Report)*, p. 251

[^cite_note-96]

On July 13, Tom Wilshire, a CIA agent assigned to the FBI's international terrorism division, emailed his superiors at the CIA's [Counterterrorism Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterterrorism_Center) (CTC) requesting permission to inform the FBI that Hazmi was in the country and that Mihdhar had a U.S. visa. The CIA never responded.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006340-97]

The same day, Margarette Gillespie, an FBI analyst working in the [CTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterterrorism_Mission_Center), was told to review material about the Malaysia meeting. She was not told of the participant's presence in the U.S. The CIA gave Gillespie surveillance photos of Mihdhar and Hazmi from the meeting to show to FBI counterterrorism but did not tell her their significance. The Intelink database informed her not to share intelligence material with criminal investigators. When shown the photos, the FBI refused more details on their significance, and they were not given Mihdhar's date of birth or passport number.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006340–343-98] In late August 2001, Gillespie told the [INS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_and_Naturalization_Service), the State Department, the [Customs Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Customs_Service), and the FBI to put Hazmi and Mihdhar on their watchlists, but the FBI was prohibited from using criminal agents in searching for the duo, hindering their efforts.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006352–353-99]

Also in July, a [Phoenix](49121.md)-based FBI agent sent a message to FBI headquarters, Alec Station, and FBI agents in New York alerting them to "the possibility of a coordinated effort by Osama bin Laden to send students to the United States to attend civil aviation universities and colleges." The agent, Kenneth Williams, suggested the need to interview flight school managers and identify all Arab students seeking flight training.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006350-100] In July, Jordan alerted the U.S. that al-Qaeda was planning an attack on the U.S.; "months later", Jordan notified the U.S. that the attack's codename was "The Big Wedding" and that it involved airplanes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYitzhak2016218-101]

On August 6, 2001, the CIA's Presidential Daily Brief, designated "For the President Only", was entitled *[Bin Ladin Determined To Strike in US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bin_Ladin_Determined_To_Strike_in_US)*. The memo noted that FBI information "indicates patterns of suspicious activity in this country consistent with preparations for hijackings or other types of attacks."[^cite_note-102]

In mid-August, one [Minnesota](19590.md) flight school alerted the FBI about [Zacarias Moussaoui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zacarias_Moussaoui), who had asked "suspicious questions." The FBI found that Moussaoui was a radical who had traveled to Pakistan, and the INS arrested him for overstaying his French visa. Their request to search his laptop was denied by FBI headquarters due to the lack of [probable cause](146751.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006350–351-103]

The failures in intelligence-sharing were attributed to 1995 [Justice Department](52563.md) policies limiting intelligence-sharing, combined with CIA and NSA reluctance to reveal "sensitive sources and methods" such as tapped phones.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWright2006342–343-104] Testifying before the [9/11 Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Commission) in April 2004, then-[Attorney General](44002.md) [John Ashcroft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Ashcroft) recalled that the "single greatest structural cause for the September 11th problem was the wall that segregated or separated criminal investigators and intelligence agents."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJavorsekRoseMarshallLeitner2015742-105] Clarke also wrote: "\[T]here were ... failures to get information to the right place at the right time."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarke2004238-106]

## Attacks

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline for the day of the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_for_the_day_of_the_September_11_attacks).

Early on the morning of September 11, 2001, [nineteen hijackers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hijackers_in_the_September_11_attacks) took control of four commercial airliners (two [Boeing 757s](151995.md) and two [Boeing 767s](4165.md)).[^cite_note-911-ch1-107] Large planes with long flights were selected for hijacking because they would have more fuel.[^cite_note-Chap7-108] Nobody on the planes survived.

### Crashes

See also: [Media documentation of the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_documentation_of_the_September_11_attacks) and [Air traffic control during the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_traffic_control_during_the_September_11_attacks)



The North Tower (right) shortly after impact from [American Airlines Flight 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines_Flight_11), the day's first attack



The South Tower (left) shortly after impact from [United Airlines Flight 175](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines_Flight_175), the day's second attack

Security camera footage of [American Airlines Flight 77](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines_Flight_77) crashing into [the Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon), the third attack.

[^cite_note-jw-117]

&#x20;The plane collides with the Pentagon approximately 86 seconds after the start of the recording.



The crash site of [United Airlines Flight 93](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines_Flight_93), near [Stonycreek Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonycreek_Township,_Somerset_County,_Pennsylvania) shortly after the crash, the final attack

At 7\:59 a.m., [American Airlines Flight 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines_Flight_11) took off from [Logan International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logan_International_Airport) in [Boston](24437894.md).[^cite_note-118] About fifteen minutes into the flight, five hijackers armed with [boxcutters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxcutter) took over the plane, injuring at least three people and possibly killing [one](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Lewin),[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121] before forcing their way into the cockpit. The terrorists also displayed an apparent explosive and sprayed [mace](1202646.md) into the cabin, to frighten the hostages into submission and further hinder resistance.[^cite_note-122] Back at Logan, [United Airlines Flight 175](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines_Flight_175) took off at 8\:14 a.m.[^cite_note-We_have_some_planes-123] Hundreds of miles southwest at [Dulles International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dulles_International_Airport), [American Airlines Flight 77](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines_Flight_77) left the runway at 8\:20 a.m.[^cite_note-We_have_some_planes-123]

Flight 175's journey proceeded normally for 28 minutes until 8\:42 am, when a group of five hijacked the plane, murdering both pilots and stabbing several crew members before assuming control of the aircraft. These hijackers also used bomb threats to instill fear into the passengers and crew,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE9/11_Commission2004a7–8-124] also spraying "tear gas, pepper spray or another irritant" in the cabin to force passengers and flight attendants to the rear of the cabin.[^cite_note-125] Concurrently, [United Airlines Flight 93](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines_Flight_93) departed from [Newark International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark_International_Airport) in [New Jersey](21648.md);[^cite_note-We_have_some_planes-123] originally scheduled to pull away from the gate at 8\:00 a.m., the plane was running 42 minutes late.[^cite_note-National_Park_Service-126]

At 8\:46 a.m., Flight 11 was deliberately crashed into the north face of the World Trade Center's North Tower between the 93rd and 99th floors.[^cite_note-127] The initial presumption by many was that it was an accident.[^cite_note-128] At 8\:51 a.m., American Airlines Flight 77 was also taken over by five hijackers who forcibly entered the cockpit 31 minutes after take-off.[^cite_note-129] Although they were equipped with knives,[^cite_note-130] there were no reports of anyone on board being stabbed, nor did the two people who made phone calls mention the use of mace or a bomb threat. Flight 175 was flown into the South Tower's southern facade (2 WTC) between the 77th and 85th floors[^cite_note-131] at 9\:03 a.m.,[^cite_note-Time-20] demonstrating that the first crash was a deliberate act of terrorism.[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133]

Four men aboard Flight 93 struck suddenly, killing at least one passenger, after having waited 46 minutes—a holdup that proved disastrous for the terrorists when combined with the delayed takeoff.[^cite_note-134] They stormed the cockpit and seized control of the plane at 9\:28 a.m., turning the plane eastbound towards Washington, D.C.[^cite_note-135] Much like their counterparts on the first two flights, the fourth team used bomb threats and filled the cabin with mace.[^cite_note-136]

Nine minutes after Flight 93 was hijacked, Flight 77 crashed into the west side of the Pentagon at 9\:37 a.m.[^cite_note-137] Because of the two delays,[^cite_note-138] the passengers and crew of Flight 93 had time to learn of the previous attacks through phone calls to the ground, and, as a result, an uprising was hastily organized to take control of the aircraft at 9\:57 a.m.[^cite_note-WP93-139] Passengers and crew began a counterattack to retake control of the aircraft. As the revolt reached the cockpit door, the hijacker-pilot began violently pitching and rolling the aircraft to disrupt the assault. The struggle continued until 10\:03\:11 a.m., when the aircraft crashed into a field in [Stonycreek Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonycreek_Township,_Somerset_County,_Pennsylvania), near Shanksville, Pennsylvania, following a final roll onto its back, failing to reach the hijackers' intended target in Washington, D.C.[^cite_note-Flight93Xscript-140][^cite_note-NPS_Flight_93_Story-141] The plane was about twenty minutes away from reaching D.C. at the time of the crash, and its target is believed to have been either the [Capitol Building](31979.md) or the [White House](33057.md).[^cite_note-Chap7-108][^cite_note-WP93-139]

Some passengers and crew who called from the aircraft using the cabin air phone service and mobile phones provided details: several hijackers were aboard each plane; they used [mace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mace_\(spray\)), tear gas, or pepper spray to overcome attendants; and some people aboard had been stabbed.[^cite_note-93phone-142] Reports indicated hijackers stabbed and killed pilots, flight attendants, and one or more passengers.[^cite_note-911-ch1-107][^cite_note-143] According to the 9/11 Commission's final report, the hijackers had recently purchased multi-function hand tools and assorted [Leatherman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leatherman)-type utility knives with locking blades, which were not forbidden to passengers at the time, but these were not found among the possessions left behind by the hijackers.[^cite_note-commission-144][^cite_note-145]

A flight attendant on Flight 11, a passenger on Flight 175, and passengers on Flight 93 said the hijackers had bombs, but one of the passengers said he thought the bombs were fake. The FBI found no traces of explosives at the crash sites, and the 9/11 Commission concluded that the bombs were probably fake.[^cite_note-911-ch1-107] On at least two of the hijacked flights—American 11 and United 93—the terrorists claimed over the PA system that they were taking hostages and were returning to the airport to have a ransom demand met, which the 9/11 Commission concluded was intended to prevent passengers from resisting. Both attempts failed, however, as both hijacker pilots in these instances (Atta[^cite_note-146] and Jarrah,[^cite_note-147] respectively) mistakenly transmitted their messages to ATC instead of the people on the plane as intended, tipping off the flight controllers that the planes had been hijacked.



An image taken from the [International Space Station](15043.md) showing the extent of the smoke plume

[Three buildings in the World Trade Center collapsed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collapse_of_the_World_Trade_Center) due to fire-induced structural failure. Although the South Tower was struck around seventeen minutes after the North Tower, the plane's impact zone was far lower, at a much faster speed, and into a corner, with the unevenly-balanced additional structural weight causing it to collapse first at 9\:59 a.m.,[^cite_note-auto-148]<sup>: 80 </sup>[^cite_note-finalreport-149]<sup>: 322 </sup> having burned for 56 minutes[^cite_note-150] in the fire caused by the crash of United Airlines Flight 175 and the explosion of its fuel. The North Tower lasted just under 29 and a half minutes longer, before collapsing at 10\:28 a.m.,[^cite_note-153] just under one hour and forty-two minutes[^cite_note-Time1-25] after being struck by American Airlines Flight 11.&#x20;

When the North Tower collapsed, debris fell on the nearby [7 World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7_World_Trade_Center_\(1987%E2%80%932001\)) building (7 WTC), damaging the building and starting fires. These fires burned for nearly seven hours, compromising the building's structural integrity, and 7 WTC collapsed at 5\:21 p.m.[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Final_Report_on_the_Collapse_of_World_Trade_Center_Building_7''2008xxxvii-155] The west side of the Pentagon sustained significant damage.[^cite_note-156] At 9\:42 a.m., the [Federal Aviation Administration](11186.md) (FAA) grounded all civilian aircraft within the continental U.S., and civilian aircraft already in flight were told to land immediately.[^cite_note-157] All international civilian aircraft were either turned back or redirected to airports in Canada or Mexico, and were banned from landing on [United States territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_territory) for three days.[^cite_note-158]

The attacks created widespread confusion among news organizations and air traffic controllers. Among unconfirmed and often contradictory news reports aired throughout the day, one of the most prevalent claimed a car bomb had been detonated at the U.S. State Department's headquarters in Washington, D.C.[^cite_note-errors2-159], but the report was retracted.[^cite_note-160] Another jet ([Delta Air Lines Flight 1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_Air_Lines_Flight_1989)) was suspected of having been hijacked, but the aircraft responded to controllers and landed safely in Cleveland, Ohio.[^cite_note-161]

Korean Air flight 085 diverted to Whitehorse, Yukon, Canada, surrounded by police snipers and Whitehorse airport and downtown evacuated after it responded to air traffic control to transmit a hijacking code but air traffic control interpreted it to mean an actual hijack and not merely a response to air traffic request.[^cite_note-162] Prime Minister [Jean Chretien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Chretien) of Canada had given the authorization to shoot down the Korean Air Boeing 747 if necessary.[^cite_note-163] A single exception to the FAA's grounding order came on September 12, when a flight was authorized from Fort Lauderdale, to San Diego, and on to Miami to retrieve a rare [antivenom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antivenom) for a professional snake handler who had been bitten by an [inland taipan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inland_taipan).[^cite_note-164]

In an April 2002 interview, Mohammed and bin al-Shibh, who are believed to have organized the attacks, said Flight 93's intended target was the United States Capitol, not the White House.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoudaFielding2004158–159-165] During the planning stage of the attacks, Atta thought the White House might be too tough a target and sought an assessment from Hanjour.[^cite_note-The_Eleventh_Day_pp_323-166] Mohammed said al-Qaeda initially planned to target nuclear installations rather than the World Trade Center and the Pentagon, but decided against it, fearing things could "get out of control."[^cite_note-AlQaedaplotted2002-167] Final decisions on targets, according to Mohammed, were left in the hands of the pilots.[^cite_note-The_Eleventh_Day_pp_323-166] If any pilot could not reach his intended target, he was to crash the plane.[^cite_note-Chap7-108]

### Casualties

Main articles: [Casualties of the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_September_11_attacks) and [Lists of victims of the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lists_of_victims_of_the_September_11_attacks)

The attack on the World Trade Center's North Tower alone[^cite_note-169] made 9/11 the deadliest act of terrorism in history.[^cite_note-ourworldindata-terrorism-2996-170] Taken together, the four crashes killed 2,996 people (including the hijackers) and injured thousands more.[^cite_note-CBC-Winnipegger-171] The death toll included 265 on the four planes (from which there were no survivors); 2,606 in the World Trade Center and the surrounding area; and 125 at the Pentagon.[^cite_note-edition.cnn.com-172][^cite_note-173] Most who died were civilians, as well as 343 firefighters, 72 law enforcement officers, 55 military personnel, and the 19 terrorists.[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175] More than 90 countries lost citizens in the attacks.[^cite_note-countries_deaths-176]

In New York City, more than 90% of those who died in the towers had been at or above the points of impact. In the North Tower, between 1,344[^cite_note-177] and 1,402 people were at, above or one floor below the point of impact and all died. Hundreds were killed instantly when the plane struck.[^cite_note-178] The estimated 800 people[^cite_note-102Mins-179] who survived the impact were trapped and died in the fires or from smoke inhalation, fell or jumped from the tower to escape the smoke and flames, or were killed in the building's collapse. The destruction of all three staircases in the North Tower when Flight 11 hit made it impossible for anyone from the impact zone upward to escape. 107 people not trapped by the impact died.[^cite_note-NISTcasualties-180] When Flight 11 struck between floors 93 and 99, the 92nd floor was rendered inescapable: the crash severed all elevator shafts while falling debris blocked the stairwells, ensuring the deaths of all 69 workers on the floor.[^cite_note-181]

In the South Tower, around 600 people were on or above the 77th floor when Flight 175 struck; few survived. As with the North Tower, hundreds were killed at the moment of impact. Unlike those in the North Tower, the estimated 300 survivors[^cite_note-102Mins-179] of the crash were not technically trapped, but most were either unaware that a means of escape still existed or were unable to use it. One stairway, [Stairwell A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stairwell_A), narrowly avoided being destroyed, allowing 14 people located on the floors of impact, including [Stanley Praimnath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Praimnath), a man who saw the plane coming at him, and four more from the floors above to escape. New York City [9-1-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9-1-1) operators who received calls from people inside the tower were not well informed of the situation as it rapidly unfolded and as a result, told callers not to descend the tower on their own.[^cite_note-182] In total, 630 people died in the South Tower, fewer than half the number killed in the North Tower.[^cite_note-NISTcasualties-180] Of the 100–200 people witnessed jumping or falling to their deaths,[^cite_note-183] only three recorded sightings were from the South Tower.[^cite_note-auto-148]<sup>: 86 </sup>

Casualties in the South Tower were significantly reduced because some occupants decided to leave the building immediately following the first crash, and because Eric Eisenberg, an executive at [AON Insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aon_\(company\)), decided to evacuate the floors occupied by AON (92 and 98–105) following the impact of Flight 11. The 17-minute gap allowed more than 900 of the 1,100 AON employees present to evacuate from above the 77th floor before the South Tower was struck. Eisenberg was among the nearly 200 who did not escape. Similar pre-impact evacuations were carried out by [Fiduciary Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiduciary_Trust_Company_International), [CSC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporation_Service_Company), and Euro Brokers, all of whom had offices on floors above the point of impact. The failure to order a full evacuation of the South Tower after the first plane crash into the North Tower was described by *[USA Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Today)* as "one of the day's great tragedies."[^cite_note-Purpura_2007_p300-184]

As exemplified in the photograph *[The Falling Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Falling_Man)*, more than 200 people fell to their deaths from the burning towers, most of whom were forced to [jump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumper_\(suicide\)) to escape the extreme heat, fire and smoke.[^cite_note-horrificdecision-185] Some occupants of each tower above the point of impact made their way toward the roof in the hope of helicopter rescue, but the roof access doors were locked.[^cite_note-186] No plan existed for helicopter rescues, and the combination of roof equipment, thick smoke and intense heat prevented helicopters from approaching.[^cite_note-187]

At the World Trade Center complex, 414 emergency workers died as they tried to rescue people and fight fires. Another law enforcement officer was killed when United 93 crashed. 343 firefighters of the [New York City Fire Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Fire_Department) (FDNY) died, including a [chaplain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mychal_Judge) and two [paramedics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramedics).[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190] 23 officers of [New York City Police Department](255413.md) (NYPD) died.[^cite_note-191] 37 officers of the [Port Authority Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_Police_Department) (PAPD) died.[^cite_note-192] Eight emergency medical technicians and paramedics from private emergency medical services units were killed.[^cite_note-193] Almost all of the emergency personnel who died at the scene were killed as a result of the towers collapsing, with the exception of one who was struck by a civilian falling from the South Tower.[^cite_note-194]

658 employees from [Cantor Fitzgerald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantor_Fitzgerald) L.P., an investment bank on the North Tower's 101st–105th floors, died, considerably more than any other employer.[^cite_note-195] 358 employees from [Marsh Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsh_Inc.), located immediately below Cantor Fitzgerald on floors 93–100, died.[^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197] 176 employees from [Aon Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aon_Corporation) died.[^cite_note-198] The [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Standards_and_Technology) (NIST) estimated that about 17,400 civilians were in the World Trade Center complex at the time of the attacks.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAverill2005"Occupant_Behavior,_Egress,_and_Emergency_Communications"-199]<sup>: xxxiii </sup> Turnstile counts from the Port Authority suggest 14,154 people were typically in the Twin Towers by 8\:45 a.m.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwyerFlynn2005266-200] Most people below the impact zone safely evacuated.[^cite_note-201]

In [Arlington County, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia), 125 Pentagon workers died when Flight 77 crashed into the building's western side. Seventy were civilians and 55 were military personnel, many of whom worked for the [United States Army](32087.md) or the [United States Navy](20518076.md). 47 civilian employees, six civilian contractors, and 22 soldiers working for the Army died, while six civilian employees, three civilian contractors, and 33 sailors working for the Navy died. Seven [Defense Intelligence Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Intelligence_Agency) (DIA) civilian employees and one [Office of the Secretary of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_Secretary_of_Defense) contractor died.[^cite_note-national-202][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldberg_et_al.2007208–212-203][^cite_note-204] [Timothy Maude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timothy_Maude), a [Lieutenant General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lieutenant_General) and Army Deputy Chief of Staff, was the highest-ranking military official killed at the Pentagon.[^cite_note-arlington-205]

Weeks after the attack, the death toll was estimated to be over 6,000, more than twice the number of deaths eventually confirmed.[^cite_note-206] The city was only able to identify remains for about 1,600 of the World Trade Center victims. The medical examiner's office collected "about 10,000 unidentified bone and tissue fragments that cannot be matched to the list of the dead."[^cite_note-CBS2-207] Bone fragments were still being found in 2006 by workers who were preparing to demolish the damaged [Deutsche Bank Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Bank_Building).[^cite_note-208]

In 2010, a team of anthropologists and archaeologists searched for human remains and personal items at the [Fresh Kills Landfill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fresh_Kills_Landfill), where 72 more human remains were recovered, bringing the total found to 1,845. As of 2011, DNA profiling was ongoing in an attempt to identify additional victims.[^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210][^cite_note-DNAContinue-211] In 2014, three coffin-size cases carrying 7,930 unidentified remains were transferred to a medical examiner's repository located at the same site as the [National September 11 Memorial & Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_September_11_Memorial_&_Museum).[^cite_note-212] Victims' families are permitted to visit a private "reflection room" which is closed to the public. The choice to place the remains in an underground area attached to a museum has been controversial; families of some victims have attempted to have the remains instead interred in a separate, above-ground monument.[^cite_note-213]

In August 2017, the 1,641st victim was identified as a result of newly available DNA technology,[^cite_note-214] and a 1,642nd during July 2018.[^cite_note-no1642-215] Three more victims were identified in October 2019,[^cite_note-216] two in September 2021[^cite_note-2021-Sep-217] and an additional two in September 2023.[^cite_note-\:0-218] As of 2025, 1,103 victims remain unidentified, amounting to 40% of the deaths in the World Trade Center attacks.[^cite_note-219] On September 25, 2023, the FDNY reported that the department had now lost the same number of members to 9/11-related illnesses as it did on the day of the attacks.[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221]

### Damage

Further information: [Collapse of the World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collapse_of_the_World_Trade_Center) and [List of buildings damaged or destroyed in the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_buildings_damaged_or_destroyed_in_the_September_11_attacks)

World Trade Center and the surrounding area as seen from directly above depicting where the two planes impacted the towers

A view directly above the attack on the World Trade Center. Showing the planes' position upon impact.

Diagram of the World Trade Center plane debris

Location of debris from the two planes to impact the World Trade Center



The [World Trade Center site](67173.md), referred to as [Ground Zero](67173.md), with an overlay showing the locations of the original buildings

The Twin Towers, [Marriott World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marriott_World_Trade_Center) (3 WTC), [7 WTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7_World_Trade_Center_\(1987%E2%80%932001\)), and [St. Nicholas Greek Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Nicholas_Greek_Orthodox_Church) were destroyed.[^cite_note-wtcstudy-222] The U.S. Customs House ([6 World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6_World_Trade_Center_\(1974%E2%80%932001\))), [4 World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4_World_Trade_Center_\(1975%E2%80%932001\)), [5 World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5_World_Trade_Center_\(1972%E2%80%932001\)), and both pedestrian bridges connecting buildings were severely damaged. All surrounding streets were in ruins.[^cite_note-223] The last fires at the World Trade Center site were extinguished on December 20.[^cite_note-224]

The Deutsche Bank Building was damaged and was later condemned as uninhabitable because of toxic conditions; it was deconstructed starting in 2007.[^cite_note-The_Eleventh_Day_pp_75-225][^cite_note-130_Liberty_Finally_Gone_from_Ground_Zero-226][^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228] Buildings of the [World Financial Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookfield_Place_\(New_York_City\)) were damaged.[^cite_note-The_Eleventh_Day_pp_75-225] The [Borough of Manhattan Community College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borough_of_Manhattan_Community_College)'s Fiterman Hall was condemned due to extensive damage, and then reopened in 2012.[^cite_note-229]

Other neighboring buildings (including [90 West Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/90_West_Street) and the [Verizon Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verizon_Building)) suffered major damage but have been restored.[^cite_note-nyconstruction-230] World Financial Center buildings, [One Liberty Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Liberty_Plaza), the [Millennium Hilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Downtown_New_York_Hotel), and 90 Church Street had moderate damage and have been restored.[^cite_note-231] Communications equipment on top of the North Tower was also destroyed, with only [WCBS-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WCBS-TV) maintaining a backup transmitter on the [Empire State Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_State_Building), but media stations were quickly able to reroute the signals and resume their broadcasts.[^cite_note-wtcstudy-222][^cite_note-232]

The [PATH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PATH_\(rail_system\)) train system's [World Trade Center station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_station_\(PATH\)) was located under the complex and was demolished when the towers collapsed. The tunnels leading to [Exchange Place station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exchange_Place_station_\(PATH\)) in [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City) were flooded with water.[^cite_note-233] The station was rebuilt as the \$4 billion [World Trade Center Transportation Hub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_Transportation_Hub), which reopened in March 2015.[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235] The [Cortlandt Street station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTC_Cortlandt_station) on the [New York City Subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Subway)'s [IRT Broadway–Seventh Avenue Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRT_Broadway%E2%80%93Seventh_Avenue_Line) was also in close proximity to the World Trade Center complex, and the entire station, along with the surrounding track, was reduced to rubble.[^cite_note-236] The station was rebuilt and reopened to the public on September 8, 2018.[^cite_note-237]



A September 14 aerial view of [the Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) during cleanup operations

The Pentagon was extensively damaged, causing one section of the building's E ring to collapse.[^cite_note-238] The plane hit the Pentagon at the first-floor level. The front part of the fuselage disintegrated on impact;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldberg_et_al.200717-239] debris from the tail section penetrated the furthest into the building, breaking through 310 feet (94 m) of the three outermost of the building's five rings.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldberg_et_al.200717-239][^cite_note-240]

### Rescue efforts

Main article: [Rescue and recovery effort after the September 11 attacks on the World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rescue_and_recovery_effort_after_the_September_11_attacks_on_the_World_Trade_Center)

See also: [List of emergency and first responder agencies that responded to the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_emergency_and_first_responder_agencies_that_responded_to_the_September_11_attacks)



Patrol Boat *Hocking* of the [U.S. Army Corps of Engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army_Corps_of_Engineers) on its way to assist in emergency relief efforts

The FDNY deployed more than 200 units (approximately half of the department) to the World Trade Center.[^cite_note-mckinsey-exec-241] Their efforts were supplemented by off-duty firefighters, [Hatzolah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatzolah),[^cite_note-242] and [emergency medical technicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_medical_technician).[^cite_note-mckinsey-ems-243][^cite_note-mckinsey-exec-241][^cite_note-244] The NYPD sent its [Emergency Service Units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Police_Department_Emergency_Service_Unit) and other police personnel and deployed its aviation unit,[^cite_note-245] which determined that helicopter rescues from the towers were not feasible.[^cite_note-246] Numerous police officers of the Port Authority Police Department (PAPD) also participated in rescue efforts.[^cite_note-247] Once on the scene, the FDNY, the NYPD, and the PAPD did not coordinate efforts and performed redundant searches for civilians.[^cite_note-mckinsey-ems-243][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlavosiusRodriquez2005666–680-248]

As conditions deteriorated, the NYPD aviation unit relayed information to police commanders, who issued orders for personnel to evacuate the towers; most NYPD officers were able to evacuate before the buildings collapsed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlavosiusRodriquez2005666–680-248][^cite_note-mckinsey-nypd-249] With separate command posts set up and incompatible radio communications between the agencies, warnings were not passed along to FDNY commanders.[^cite_note-250]

After the first tower collapsed, FDNY commanders issued evacuation warnings. Due to [malfunctioning radio repeater systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_communications_during_the_September_11_attacks), many firefighters never heard the evacuation orders. 9-1-1 dispatchers also received information from callers that was not passed along to commanders on the scene.[^cite_note-mckinsey-exec-241]

## Reactions

Main article: [Reactions to the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_September_11_attacks)

See also: [Timeline for September following the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_for_September_following_the_September_11_attacks)

The 9/11 attacks resulted in immediate responses, including [domestic reactions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._government_response_to_the_September_11_attacks); [closings and cancellations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closings_and_cancellations_following_the_September_11_attacks); [hate crimes](13547.md); [international responses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_September_11_attacks); and [military responses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._military_response_during_the_September_11_attacks). Shortly after the attacks, the [September 11th Victim Compensation Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11th_Victim_Compensation_Fund) was created by an [Act of Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_of_Congress).[^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252] The purpose of the fund was to compensate the victims of the attacks and their families with their agreement not to file [lawsuits](113258.md) against the airlines involved.[^cite_note-253] Legislation authorizes the fund to disburse a maximum of \$7.375 billion, including operational and administrative costs, of U.S. government funds.[^cite_note-254] The fund was set to expire by 2020 but was prolonged in 2019 to allow claims to be filed until October 2090.[^cite_note-255][^cite_note-256]

### Immediate response

Further information: [U.S. military response during the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._military_response_during_the_September_11_attacks)

See also: [Communication during the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communication_during_the_September_11_attacks)



President [George W. Bush](3414021.md) is briefed in [Sarasota, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarasota,_Florida), where he learned of the attacks unfolding while [visiting the Emma E. Brooker elementary school during the morning.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11_elementary_school_visit_by_George_W._Bush)

Eight hours after the attacks, U.S. Secretary of Defense [Donald Rumsfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Rumsfeld) declares "The Pentagon is functioning"

At 8\:32 a.m., [FAA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FAA) officials were notified Flight 11 had been hijacked, and they notified the [North American Aerospace Defense Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_Aerospace_Defense_Command) (NORAD). NORAD scrambled two [F-15s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-15s) from [Otis Air National Guard Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otis_Air_National_Guard_Base) in Massachusetts; they were airborne by 8\:53 a.m. Because of slow and confused communication from FAA officials, NORAD had nine minutes' notice, and no notice about any of the other flights before they crashed.[^cite_note-257]

After both of the Twin Towers had been hit, more fighters were scrambled from [Langley Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Base_Langley%E2%80%93Eustis) in Virginia at 9\:30 a.m.[^cite_note-norad-258] At 10\:20 am, Vice President [Dick Cheney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Cheney) issued orders to shoot down any commercial aircraft that could be positively identified as being hijacked. These instructions were not relayed in time for the fighters to take action.[^cite_note-norad-258][^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] Some fighters took to the air without live ammunition, knowing that to prevent the hijackers from striking their intended targets, the pilots might have to intercept and crash their fighters into the hijacked planes, possibly ejecting at the last moment.[^cite_note-261]

For the first time in U.S. history, the emergency preparedness plan [Security Control of Air Traffic and Air Navigation Aids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Control_of_Air_Traffic_and_Air_Navigation_Aids) (SCATANA) was invoked,[^cite_note-262] stranding tens of thousands of passengers across the world.[^cite_note-Commission-263] [Ben Sliney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Sliney), in his first day as the National Operations Manager of the FAA,[^cite_note-264] ordered that American airspace be closed to all international flights, causing about 500 flights to be turned back or redirected to other countries. Canada received 226 of the diverted flights and launched [Operation Yellow Ribbon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Yellow_Ribbon) to deal with the large numbers of grounded planes and stranded passengers.[^cite_note-canadaflights-265]

The 9/11 attacks had immediate effects on the American people.[^cite_note-266] Police and rescue workers from around the country traveled to New York City to help recover bodies from the remnants of the Twin Towers.[^cite_note-267] More than 3,000 children lost a parent in the attacks.[^cite_note-268] Blood donations across the U.S. surged in the weeks after 9/11.[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270]

### Domestic reactions

Further information: [U.S. government response to the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._government_response_to_the_September_11_attacks)

President Bush addressing the nation from the [White House](33057.md) at 8\:30 pm ET

Bush speaking to rescue workers at [Ground Zero](67173.md) on September 14 next to firefighter [Bob Beckwith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Beckwith)



During a speech to a joint session of Congress, President George W. Bush pledges "to defend freedom against terrorism". September 20, 2001 (audio only)

Following the attacks, Bush's approval rating increased to 90%.[^cite_note-271] On September 20, he addressed the nation and a joint session of Congress regarding the events, the rescue and recovery efforts, and his intended response to the attacks. [New York City mayor Rudy Giuliani's highly visible role](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudy_Giuliani_during_the_September_11_attacks) resulted in praise in New York and nationally.[^cite_note-272]

Many relief funds were immediately set up to provide [financial assistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_assistance_following_the_September_11_attacks) to the [survivors of the attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_September_11_attacks) and the victims' families. By the deadline for victims' compensation on September 11, 2003, 2,833 applications had been received from the families of those killed.[^cite_note-273] Contingency plans for the [continuity of government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuity_of_government) and the evacuation of leaders were implemented soon after the attacks.[^cite_note-Commission-263] Congress was not told that the United States had been under a continuity of government status until February 2002.[^cite_note-274] In the largest restructuring of the U.S. government in contemporary history, the United States enacted the [Homeland Security Act of 2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeland_Security_Act_of_2002), creating the [U.S. Department of Homeland Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Department_of_Homeland_Security). Congress also passed the [USA PATRIOT Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_PATRIOT_Act), saying it would help detect and prosecute terrorism and other crimes.[^cite_note-275] Civil liberties groups have criticized the PATRIOT Act, saying it allows law enforcement to invade citizens' privacy and that it eliminates judicial oversight of law enforcement and domestic intelligence.[^cite_note-ACLUAdv-276][^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278]

To effectively combat future acts of terrorism, the [National Security Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Agency) (NSA) was given broad powers. The NSA commenced [warrantless surveillance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSA_warrantless_surveillance_controversy) of telecommunications, which was sometimes criticized as permitting the agency "to eavesdrop on telephone and e-mail communications between the United States and people overseas without a warrant."[^cite_note-279] In response to requests by intelligence agencies, the [United States Foreign Intelligence Surveillance Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Foreign_Intelligence_Surveillance_Court) permitted an expansion of powers by the U.S. government in seeking, obtaining, and sharing information on U.S. citizens, as well as non-Americans around the world.[^cite_note-280]

#### Hate crimes

See also: [Islamophobia in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobia_in_the_United_States)

Six days after the attacks, President Bush made a public appearance at [Washington, D.C.'s largest Islamic Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Center_of_Washington) where he acknowledged the "incredibly valuable contribution" of [American Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Muslims) and called for them "to be treated with respect."[^cite_note-281] Numerous incidents of harassment and hate crimes against Muslims and [South Asians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asians) were reported in the days following the attacks.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283][^cite_note-Hate-284]

[Sikhs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikh) were also targeted due to their use of [turbans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turban), which are stereotypically associated with Muslims. There were reports of attacks on mosques and other religious buildings (including the firebombing of a [Hindu temple](2247213.md)), and assaults on individuals, including one murder: [Balbir Singh Sodhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balbir_Singh_Sodhi), a Sikh mistaken for a Muslim, who was fatally shot on September 15, 2001, in [Mesa, Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesa,_Arizona).[^cite_note-Hate-284] Two dozen members of Osama bin Laden's family were urgently evacuated out of the country on a private charter plane under FBI supervision three days after the attacks.[^cite_note-285]

According to an academic study, people perceived to be [Middle Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Eastern) were as likely to be victims of hate crimes as followers of Islam during this time. The study also found a similar increase in hate crimes against people who may have been perceived as Muslims, Arabs, and others thought to be of Middle Eastern origin.[^cite_note-286] A report by the South Asian American advocacy group South Asian Americans Leading Together documented media coverage of 645 bias incidents against Americans of South Asian or Middle Eastern descent between September 11 and 17, 2001. Crimes such as vandalism, arson, assault, shootings, harassment, and threats in numerous places were documented.[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288] Women wearing the [hijab](68301.md) were also targeted.[^cite_note-HRW_not_enemy-289]

#### Discrimination and racial profiling

Further information: [Detentions following the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detentions_following_the_September_11_attacks) and [Flying while Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flying_while_Muslim)

See also: [Airport racial profiling in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airport_racial_profiling_in_the_United_States)

A poll of [Arab-Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab-Americans) in May 2002 found that 20% had personally experienced discrimination since September 11. A July 2002 poll of Muslim Americans found that 48% believed their lives had changed for the worse since September 11, and 57% had experienced an act of bias or discrimination.[^cite_note-HRW_not_enemy-289] Following the September 11 attacks, many [Pakistani Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistani_Americans) identified themselves as Indians to avoid potential discrimination and obtain jobs.[^cite_note-290]

By May 2002, there were 488 complaints of [employment discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Employment_discrimination_in_the_United_States) reported to the U.S. Equal Employment Opportunity Commission (EEOC). 301 of those were complaints from people fired from their jobs. Similarly, by June 2002, the U.S. Department of Transportation (DOT) had investigated 111 September 11th-related complaints from airline passengers purporting that their religious or ethnic appearance caused them to be singled out at security screenings in addition to 31 complaints from people who alleged they were blocked from boarding airplanes on the same grounds.[^cite_note-HRW_not_enemy-289]

#### Muslim American response

See also: [Muslim attitudes toward terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_attitudes_toward_terrorism) and [Peace in Islamic philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_in_Islamic_philosophy)

Muslim organizations in the United States were swift to condemn the attacks and called "upon [Muslim Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Americans) to come forward with their skills and resources to help alleviate the sufferings of the affected people and their families."[^cite_note-291] These organizations included the [Islamic Society of North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Society_of_North_America), American Muslim Alliance, [American Muslim Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Muslim_Council), [Council on American-Islamic Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_on_American-Islamic_Relations), [Islamic Circle of North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Circle_of_North_America), and the Shari'a Scholars Association of North America. Along with monetary donations, many Islamic organizations launched blood drives and provided medical assistance, food, and shelter for victims.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293][^cite_note-294]

#### Interfaith efforts

Curiosity about Islam increased after the attacks. As a result, many mosques and Islamic centers began holding open houses and participating in outreach efforts to educate non-Muslims about the faith. In the first 10 years after the attacks, [interfaith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interfaith_dialogue) community service increased from 8 to 20 percent, and the percentage of U.S. congregations involved in interfaith worship doubled from 7 to 14 percent.[^cite_note-295]

### International reactions

The attacks were denounced by mass media and governments worldwide. Nations offered pro-American support and solidarity.[^cite_note-296] Leaders in most Middle Eastern countries, as well as Libya and Afghanistan, condemned the attacks. Iraq was a notable exception, with an immediate official statement that "the American cowboys are reaping the fruit of their crimes against humanity."[^cite_note-297] The government of Saudi Arabia officially condemned the attacks, but privately many Saudis favored bin Laden's cause.[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-The_Kingdom_and_the_Towers-299]

Although [Palestinian Authority](24093.md) (PA) president [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat) also condemned the attacks, there were reports of celebrations of disputed size in the [West Bank](33209.md), [Gaza Strip](12047.md),[^cite_note-\:6-300] and [East Jerusalem](343508.md).[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302] Arafat ordered his security services to break up the celebrations and tried to obstruct news organizations from showing them.[^cite_note-\:5-303][^cite_note-\:6-300][^cite_note-304] The PA claimed that such celebrations do not represent Palestinian sentiment overall.[^cite_note-\:5-303] Footage by CNN and other news outlets were suggested by a report originating at a Brazilian university to be from 1991; this was later proven to be a false accusation.[^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306] As in the United States, the aftermath of the attacks saw tensions increase in other countries between Muslims and non-Muslims.[^cite_note-307]

[United Nations Security Council Resolution 1368](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1368) condemned the attacks and expressed readiness to take all necessary steps to respond and combat terrorism in accordance with their [Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapter_VII_of_the_United_Nations_Charter).[^cite_note-SecCounc-308] Numerous countries introduced anti-terrorism legislation and froze bank accounts they suspected of al-Qaeda ties.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] Law enforcement and intelligence agencies in a number of countries arrested alleged terrorists.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312]
