British Prime Minister [Tony Blair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Blair) said Britain stood "shoulder to shoulder" with the United States.[^cite_note-313] In a speech to Congress nine days after the attacks, which Blair attended as a guest, President Bush declared "America has no truer friend than Great Britain."[^cite_note-314] Subsequently, Prime Minister Blair embarked on two months of diplomacy to rally international support for military action; he held 54 meetings with world leaders.[^cite_note-315]

The U.S. set up the [Guantanamo Bay detention camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guantanamo_Bay_detention_camp) to hold inmates they defined as "[illegal enemy combatants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal_enemy_combatants)." The legitimacy of these detentions has been questioned by the [European Union](9317.md) and human rights organizations.[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318]

On September 25, 2001, [Iran](14653.md)'s president [Mohammad Khatami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Khatami), meeting British Foreign Secretary [Jack Straw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Straw), said: "Iran fully understands the feelings of the Americans about the terrorist attacks in New York and Washington on September 11." He said although the American administrations had been at best indifferent about terrorist operations in Iran, the Iranians felt differently and had expressed their sympathetic feelings with bereaved Americans in the tragic incidents in the two cities. He also stated that "Nations should not be punished in place of terrorists."[^cite_note-319]

According to [Radio Farda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Farda)'s website, when the news of the attacks was released, some Iranian citizens gathered in front of the Embassy of Switzerland in Tehran, which serves as the protecting power of the United States in Iran, to express their sympathy, and some of them lit candles as a symbol of mourning. Radio Farda's website also states that in 2011, on the anniversary of the attacks, the [United States Department of State](31975.md) published a post on its blog, in which the Department thanked the Iranian people for their sympathy and stated that it would never forget Iranian people's kindness.[^cite_note-320] After the attacks, both the President[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] and the Supreme Leader of Iran condemned the attacks. The [BBC](19344654.md) and *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* magazine published reports on holding candlelit vigils for the victims by Iranian citizens on their websites.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324] According to *[Politico Magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politico_Magazine)*, following the attacks, [Ali Khamenei](385653.md), the Supreme Leader of Iran, "suspended the usual '[Death to America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_America)' chants at [Friday prayers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friday_prayers)" temporarily.[^cite_note-Politico_2013-11-19-325]

### Military operations

Further information: [War on terror](13425800.md) and [United States invasion of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Afghanistan)



An Army National Guard soldier and an FDNY firefighter searching debris at the World Trade Center site on September 18, 2001.

At 2\:40 pm on September 11, Secretary of Defense [Donald Rumsfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Rumsfeld) was issuing orders to his aides to look for evidence of Iraqi involvement. According to notes taken by senior policy official [Stephen Cambone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Cambone), Rumsfeld asked for, "Best info fast. Judge whether they are good enough to hit [S.H.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein) at the same time. Not only [OBL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_Bin_Laden)."[^cite_note-326] Hours after the attacks, [New York Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Governor) [George Pataki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Pataki) mobilized elements of the [New York National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Army_National_Guard) and ordered them to [lower Manhattan](455646.md). By nightfall, soldiers from the [42nd Infantry Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/42nd_Infantry_Division_\(United_States\)) arrived at the WTC site and established a security perimeter around the piles of rubble that had once been the WTC complex. The 42nd Division later assumed command of the [New York National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_National_Guard) joint task force for response and recovery in lower Manhattan.[^cite_note-327]

The [District of Columbia Air National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_of_Columbia_Air_National_Guard)'s [113th Wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/113th_Wing) at [Andrews Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrews_Air_Force_Base) was not part of the [North American Aerospace Defense Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_Aerospace_Defense_Command) (NORAD) network tasked with domestic airspace monitoring. Following a direct inquiry to the [Secret Service](58221.md) regarding airspace restrictions, the Secret Service bypassed the standard military chain of command to request an immediate fighter scramble from the [White House](33057.md).[^cite_note-328]

[Lt. Col.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lt._Col.) [Marc Sasseville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Sasseville) and [1st Lt.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_lieutenant) [Heather Penney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heather_Penney) scrambled their [F-16s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-16s) without ammunition or live missiles, operating under ad-hoc orders to protect the capital using whatever force necessary—including intentional mid-air ramming maneuvers if United Airlines Flight 93 threatened the [U.S. Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Capitol) or White House. Later that afternoon, these air defense patrols over Washington, D.C., and New York City were formalized under the newly launched **[Operation Noble Eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Noble_Eagle)**.[^cite_note-329]

In a meeting at [Camp David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_David) on September 15 the Bush administration rejected the idea of attacking [Iraq](26215470.md) in response to the September 11 attacks.[^cite_note-330] Nonetheless, they later [invaded the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_Invasion_of_Iraq) with allies, citing "[Saddam Hussein's support for terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam%E2%80%93al-Qaeda_conspiracy_theory)."[^cite_note-331] At the time, as many as seven in ten Americans believed the Iraqi president played a role in the 9/11 attacks.[^cite_note-332] Three years later, Bush conceded that he had not.[^cite_note-333]

The [NATO](21133.md) council declared that the terrorist attacks on the United States were an attack on all NATO nations that satisfied [Article 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty#Article_5) of the NATO charter. This marked the first invocation of Article 5, which had been written during the [Cold War](325329.md) with an attack by the Soviet Union in mind.[^cite_note-334] Australian Prime Minister [John Howard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Howard), who was in Washington, D.C., during the attacks, invoked Article IV of the [ANZUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ANZUS) treaty.[^cite_note-335] The Bush administration announced a [war on terror](13425800.md), with the stated goals of bringing bin Laden and al-Qaeda to justice and preventing the emergence of other terrorist networks.[^cite_note-336] These goals would be accomplished by imposing economic and military sanctions against states harboring terrorists, and increasing global surveillance and intelligence sharing.[^cite_note-337]

On September 14, 2001, the [U.S. Congress](31756.md) passed the [Authorization for the use of Military Force Against Terrorists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authorization_for_Use_of_Military_Force_of_2001), which grants the President the authority to use all "necessary and appropriate force" against those whom he determined "planned, authorized, committed or aided" the September 11 attacks or who harbored said persons or groups. It is still in effect.[^cite_note-338]

On October 7, 2001, the [war in Afghanistan](19666611.md) began when U.S. and British forces initiated aerial bombing campaigns targeting [Taliban](30635.md) and al-Qaeda camps, then later invaded Afghanistan with ground troops of the [Special Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Forces).[^cite_note-339] This eventually led to the overthrow of the Taliban's rule of Afghanistan with the [Fall of Kandahar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Kandahar) on December 7, by U.S.-led [coalition forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force).[^cite_note-340]

&#x20;Al-Qaeda leader Osama bin Laden, who went into hiding in the [White Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spin_Ghar), was targeted by U.S. coalition forces in the [Battle of Tora Bora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Tora_Bora),[^cite_note-BBC_News_2011-07-11-341] but he escaped across the [Pakistani border](203570.md) and remained out of sight for almost ten years.[^cite_note-BBC_News_2011-07-11-341] In an interview with [Tayseer Allouni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tayseer_Allouni) on October 21, 2001, bin Laden stated:&#x20;

> The events proved the extent of terrorism that America exercises in the world. Bush stated that the world has to be divided in two: Bush and his supporters, and any country that doesn't get into the global crusade is with the terrorists. What terrorism is clearer than this? Many governments were forced to support this "new terrorism"... America wouldn't live in security until we live it truly in Palestine. This showed the reality of America, which puts Israel's interest above its own people's interest. America won't get out of this crisis until it gets out of the Arabian Peninsula, and until it stops its support of Israel.[^cite_note-342]

## Aftermath

Main article: [Aftermath of the September 11 attacks](171761.md)

### Health issues

Main article: [Health effects arising from the September 11 attacks](7770119.md)



Survivors covered in dust after the collapse of the [World Trade towers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\)). A photograph of another dust-covered victim, [Marcy Borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcy_Borders), subsequently gained much attention.

[^cite_note-343]

[^cite_note-344]

Hundreds of thousands of tons of toxic debris containing more than 2,500 contaminants and known carcinogens were spread across Lower Manhattan when the towers collapsed.[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346] Exposure to the toxins in the debris is alleged to have contributed to [fatal or debilitating illnesses](7770119.md) among people who were at Ground Zero.[^cite_note-CNN_2007-05-24-347][^cite_note-348] The Bush administration ordered the [Environmental Protection Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_Protection_Agency) (EPA) to issue reassuring statements regarding air quality in the aftermath of the attacks, citing national security, but the EPA did not determine that air quality had returned to pre–September 11 levels until June 2002.[^cite_note-349]

Health effects extended to residents, students, and office workers in Lower Manhattan and nearby [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Manhattan).[^cite_note-350] Several deaths have been linked to the toxic dust, and victims' names were included in the World Trade Center memorial.[^cite_note-351] An estimated 18,000 people have developed illnesses as a result of the toxic dust.[^cite_note-352] There is also scientific speculation that exposure to toxic products in the air may have negative effects on fetal development.[^cite_note-353] A study of rescue workers released in April 2010 found that all those studied had impaired lung function.[^cite_note-354]

Years after the attacks, legal disputes over the costs of related illnesses were still in the court system. In 2006, a federal judge rejected New York City's refusal to pay for health costs for rescue workers, allowing for the possibility of suits against the city.[^cite_note-355] Government officials have been faulted for urging the public to return to lower Manhattan in the weeks shortly after the attacks. [Christine Todd Whitman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_Todd_Whitman), administrator of the EPA in the attacks' aftermath, was criticized by U.S. District Judge [Deborah Batts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deborah_Batts), who wrote that Whitman's assurances about air quality were "without question conscience-shocking."[^cite_note-356] Mayor Giuliani was criticized for urging financial industry personnel to return quickly to the greater [Wall Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street) area.[^cite_note-357]

The [James L. Zadroga 9/11 Health and Compensation Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_L._Zadroga_9/11_Health_and_Compensation_Act) (2010) allocated \$4.2 billion to create the [World Trade Center Health Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_Health_Program), which provides testing and treatment for people with long-term health problems related to the 9/11 attacks.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-WTCHP_faq-359] The WTC Health Program replaced preexisting 9/11-related health programs such as the Medical Monitoring and Treatment Program and the WTC Environmental Health Center program.[^cite_note-WTCHP_faq-359]

In 2020, the NYPD confirmed that 247 NYPD police officers had died due to 9/11-related illnesses. In September 2022, the FDNY confirmed that 299 firefighters had died due to 9/11-related illnesses. Both agencies believe that the death toll will rise dramatically in the coming years. The [Port Authority of New York and New Jersey Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey_Police_Department) (PAPD), the law enforcement agency with jurisdiction over the World Trade Center, confirmed that four of its police officers have died of 9/11-related illnesses. The chief of the PAPD at the time, Joseph Morris, made sure that industrial-grade respirators were provided to all PAPD police officers within 48 hours and decided that the same 30 to 40 police officers would be stationed at the World Trade Center pile, drastically lowering the number of total PAPD personnel who would be exposed to the air. The FDNY and NYPD had rotated hundreds, if not thousands, of different personnel from all over New York City to the pile without adequate respirators and breathing equipment that could have prevented future diseases.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363]

### Economic

Main article: [Economic effects of the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_effects_of_the_September_11_attacks)



[U.S. deficit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_federal_budget) and [debt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_public_debt) increases in the seven years following the attacks from 2001 to 2008

The attacks had a significant economic impact on the U.S. and world markets.[^cite_note-364] The stock exchanges did not open on September 11 and remained closed until September 17. Reopening, the [Dow Jones Industrial Average](47361.md) (DJIA) fell 684 points, or 7.1%, to 8921, a record-setting one-day point decline.[^cite_note-365] By the end of the week, the DJIA had fallen 1,369.7 points (14.3%), at the time its largest one-week point drop in history. In 2001 dollars, U.S. stocks lost US\$1.4 trillion in valuation for the week.[^cite_note-MarkDec-366]

In New York City, about 430,000 job months and US\$2.8 billion in wages were lost in the first three months after the attacks. The economic effects were mainly on the economy's export sectors.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-IAGS_cost-368][^cite_note-PEARL9/11-369] The city's GDP was estimated to have declined by US\$27.3 billion for the last three months of 2001 and all of 2002. The U.S. government provided US\$11.2 billion in immediate assistance to the [Government of New York City](531637.md) in September 2001, and US\$10.5 billion in early 2002 for economic development and infrastructure needs.[^cite_note-crs-5-370]

Also hurt were small businesses in [Lower Manhattan](455646.md) near the World Trade Center (18,000 of which were destroyed or displaced), resulting in lost jobs and wages. Assistance was provided by [Small Business Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_Business_Administration) loans; federal government Community Development Block Grants; and Economic Injury Disaster Loans.[^cite_note-crs-5-370] Some 31,900,000 square feet (2,960,000 m<sup>2</sup>) of Lower Manhattan office space was damaged or destroyed.[^cite_note-371] Many wondered whether these jobs would return, and if the damaged tax base would recover.[^cite_note-372] Studies of 9/11's economic effects show the Manhattan office real-estate market and office employment were less affected than first feared, because of the financial services industry's need for face-to-face interaction.[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374]

North American air space was closed for several days after the attacks and air travel decreased upon its reopening, leading to a nearly 20% cutback in air travel capacity, and exacerbating financial problems in the struggling U.S. airline industry.[^cite_note-375]

The September 11 attacks also led to the U.S. [wars in Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Afghanistan_\(2001%E2%80%93present\)) and [Iraq](5043324.md),[^cite_note-376] as well as additional [homeland security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeland_security) spending, totaling at least US\$5 trillion.[^cite_note-377]

### Effects in Afghanistan

Further information: [War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](19666611.md), [Aftermath of the War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aftermath_of_the_War_in_Afghanistan_\(2001%E2%80%932021\)), and [Killing of Ayman al-Zawahiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Ayman_al-Zawahiri)

> If Americans are clamouring to bomb Afghanistan back to the [Stone Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Age), they ought to know that this nation does not have so far to go. This is a post-apocalyptic place of felled cities, parched land and downtrodden people.

— [Barry Bearak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Bearak), *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, September 13, 2001

[^cite_note-TalibanPlead-378]

Most of the [Afghan population](2235.md) was already going hungry at the time of the attacks.[^cite_note-379] In the aftermath of the attacks, tens of thousands of people attempted to flee Afghanistan due to the possibility of military retaliation by the U.S. and Pakistan, already [home to many Afghan refugees](22663356.md) from previous conflicts, closed its border with Afghanistan on September 17, 2001.[^cite_note-speechto-380] Thousands of Afghans also fled to the frontier with [Tajikistan](30108.md) but were denied entry.[^cite_note-381] The Taliban leaders in Afghanistan pleaded against military action, saying "We appeal to the United States not to put Afghanistan into more misery because our people have suffered so much", referring to [two decades of conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghanistan_conflict_\(1978-present\)) and the humanitarian crisis attached to it.[^cite_note-TalibanPlead-378]

All United Nations expatriates had left Afghanistan after the attacks and no national or international aid workers were at their post. Workers were instead preparing in bordering countries like Pakistan, China and Uzbekistan to prevent a potential "humanitarian catastrophe", amid a critically low food stock for the Afghan population.[^cite_note-382] The [World Food Programme](33582.md) stopped importing wheat to Afghanistan on September 12 due to security risks.[^cite_note-383]





*From left to right*: U.S. soldiers engaged in the war on terror in Afghanistan in May 2006 • Army Major General [Chris Donahue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Donahue) left Afghanistan as the final American soldier on August 30, 2021.

[^cite_note-The_last_soldier_to_leave_Afghanistan_—_nicknamed_'Flatliner'_—_was_uniquely_prepared_for_that_moment-384]

Approximately one month after the attacks, the United States led a broad [coalition of international forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Participants_in_Operation_Enduring_Freedom) to overthrow the Taliban regime from Afghanistan for their harboring of al-Qaeda.[^cite_note-speechto-380] Though Pakistani authorities were initially reluctant to align themselves with the U.S. against the Taliban, they permitted the coalition access to their military bases, and arrested and handed over to the U.S. more than 600 suspected al-Qaeda members.[^cite_note-385]

In 2011, the U.S. and NATO under [President Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Barack_Obama) initiated a [drawdown of troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Withdrawal_of_United_States_troops_from_Afghanistan_\(2011%E2%80%932016\)) in Afghanistan finalized in 2016. During the presidencies of [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [Joe Biden](145422.md) in 2020 and 2021, the United States alongside its NATO allies [withdrew all troops from Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Withdrawal_of_United_States_troops_from_Afghanistan_\(2020%E2%80%932021\)), completing the withdrawal of all regular U.S. troops on August 30, 2021.[^cite_note-DOD_Cronk_Biden_Announces-152][^cite_note-White_House_2021-04-14-386][^cite_note-Time_2021-387] The withdrawal marked the end of the 2001–2021 war in Afghanistan. Biden said that after nearly 20 years of war, it was clear that the U.S. military could not transform Afghanistan into a modern democracy.[^cite_note-388]

### Cultural influence

Main article: [Cultural influence of the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_influence_of_the_September_11_attacks)

Further information: [List of cultural references to the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cultural_references_to_the_September_11_attacks), [Entertainment affected by the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entertainment_affected_by_the_September_11_attacks), and [Osama bin Laden in popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden_in_popular_culture)

See also: [Osama bin Laden (elephant)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden_\(elephant\))

Immediate responses to 9/11 included greater focus on home life and time spent with family, higher church attendance, and increased expressions of patriotism such as the flying of American flags.[^cite_note-Carducci2009b-389] [The radio industry responded by removing certain songs from playlists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clear_Channel_memorandum), and the attacks have subsequently been used as background, narrative, or thematic elements in [film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_films_about_the_September_11_attacks), [music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_songs_about_the_September_11_attacks), [literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_books_about_the_September_11_attacks), and [humour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humor_based_on_the_September_11_attacks). Already-running television shows as well as programs developed after 9/11 have reflected [post-9/11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-9/11) cultural concerns.[^cite_note-Pop_Culture_Guide-390]

[9/11 conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_conspiracy_theories) have become a social phenomenon, despite a lack of support from expert scientists, engineers, and historians.[^cite_note-391] 9/11 has also had a major impact on the religious faith of many individuals; for some it strengthened, to find [consolation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consolation) to cope with the loss of loved ones and overcome their grief; others started to question their faith or lose it entirely because they [could not reconcile it](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Problem_of_evil) with their view of religion.[^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393]

The [culture of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_United_States), after the attacks, is noted for heightened security and an increased demand thereof, as well as [paranoia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paranoia) and [anxiety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anxiety) regarding future terrorist attacks against most of the nation. Psychologists have also confirmed that there has been an increased amount of national anxiety in commercial air travel.[^cite_note-394] Anti-Muslim hate crimes rose nearly ten-fold in 2001 and have subsequently remained "roughly five times higher than the pre-9/11 rate."[^cite_note-395]

### Government policies towards terrorism

Further information: [War on terror](13425800.md), [Anti-terrorism legislation](4700071.md), [Airport security repercussions due to the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airport_security_repercussions_due_to_the_September_11_attacks), and [Legal issues related to the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_issues_related_to_the_September_11_attacks)

See also: [Senate Intelligence Committee report on CIA torture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Intelligence_Committee_report_on_CIA_torture)



Alleged "[extraordinary rendition](1031191.md)" illegal flights of the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA), as reported by Polish newspaper *[Rzeczpospolita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rzeczpospolita_\(newspaper\))*

[^cite_note-396]

The September 11 attacks introduced foreign terrorism as a major security issue to the U.S., as they indicated smaller states and terrorist organizations had become increasingly capable even against major global powers.[^cite_note-397] Many governments across the world passed legislation to combat terrorism as a result to the attacks.[^cite_note-398] In Germany, where several of the 9/11 terrorists had resided and taken advantage of that country's liberal asylum policies, two major anti-terrorism packages were enacted. The first removed legal loopholes that permitted terrorists to live and raise money in Germany. The second addressed the effectiveness and communication of intelligence and law enforcement.[^cite_note-399] Canada passed the [Canadian Anti-Terrorism Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Anti-Terrorism_Act), their first anti-terrorism law.[^cite_note-400] The United Kingdom passed the [Anti-terrorism, Crime and Security Act 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-terrorism,_Crime_and_Security_Act_2001) and the [Prevention of Terrorism Act 2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prevention_of_Terrorism_Act_2005).[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] New Zealand enacted the [Terrorism Suppression Act 2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_Suppression_Act_2002).[^cite_note-403]

In the United States, the [Department of Homeland Security](58236.md) was created by the Homeland Security Act of 2002 to coordinate domestic anti-terrorism efforts.[^cite_note-404] The [USA Patriot Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Patriot_Act) gave the federal government greater powers, including the authority to detain foreign terror suspects for a week without charge; to monitor terror suspects' telephone communications, e-mail, and Internet use; and to prosecute suspected terrorists without time restrictions.[^cite_note-405] The FAA ordered that airplane cockpits be reinforced with a secondary flight deck to prevent terrorists from gaining control of planes and assigned [sky marshals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_marshals) to flights.[^cite_note-406]

The [Aviation and Transportation Security Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aviation_and_Transportation_Security_Act) made the federal government, rather than airports, responsible for [airport security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airport_security). The law created the [Transportation Security Administration](172933.md) to inspect passengers and luggage, causing long delays and concern over passenger privacy.[^cite_note-Modern_World_History-407] After suspected abuses of the USA Patriot Act were [brought to light](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_surveillance_disclosures_\(2013%E2%80%93present\)) in June 2013 with articles about the collection of American call records by the NSA and the [PRISM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PRISM) program, Representative [Jim Sensenbrenner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Sensenbrenner) (of [Wisconsin](33127.md)), who introduced the Patriot Act in 2001, said that the NSA overstepped its bounds.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409]

[Criticism of the war on terror](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_the_war_on_terror) has focused on its morality, efficiency, and cost. According to a 2021 report by the [Costs of War Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costs_of_War_Project), the several post-9/11 wars participated in by the United States in its war on terror have caused the displacement, conservatively calculated, of 38 million people in Afghanistan, Pakistan, Iraq, Libya, Syria, Yemen, Somalia, and the Philippines.[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411][^cite_note-Costs_of_War-412] They estimated these wars caused the deaths of 897,000 to 929,000 people directly and cost US\$8 trillion.[^cite_note-Costs_of_War-412]

In a 2023 report, the Costs of War Project estimated that there have been between 3.6 and 3.7 million indirect deaths in the post-9/11 war zones, with the total death toll being 4.5 to 4.6 million. The report defined post-9/11 war zones as conflicts that included significant United States counter-terrorism operations since 9/11, which in addition to the wars in [Iraq](55579888.md), Afghanistan and [Pakistan](12336420.md), also includes the civil wars in [Syria](30741795.md), [Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemeni_Civil_War_\(2014%E2%80%93present\)), [Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libyan_Crisis_\(2011%E2%80%93present\)) and [Somalia](3070332.md).[^cite_note-\:2-27] The report derived its estimate of indirect deaths using a calculation from the [Geneva Declaration of Secretariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Declaration_on_Armed_Violence_and_Development) which estimates that for every person directly killed by war, four more die from the indirect consequences of war.[^cite_note-\:2-27]

The [U.S. Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Constitution) and [U.S. law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._law) prohibits the use of [torture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torture_in_the_United_States), yet such [human rights violations occurred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_violations_by_the_CIA) during the war on terror under the euphemism "[enhanced interrogation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enhanced_interrogation)."[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] In 2005, *The Washington Post* and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) (HRW) published revelations concerning CIA flights and "[black sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_black_sites)", covert prisons operated by the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA).[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416] The term "torture by proxy" is used by some critics to describe situations in which the CIA and other U.S. agencies have [transferred suspected terrorists](1031191.md) to countries known to employ torture.[^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418]

### Legal proceedings

Main articles: [Trials related to the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trials_related_to_the_September_11_attacks) and [United States v. Khalid Sheikh Mohammed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_v._Khalid_Sheikh_Mohammed)

[President Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_Obama)'s address to the U.S. after the [killing of bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_bin_Laden)\
*Also available:* Audio only

As all 19 hijackers died in the attacks, they were never prosecuted. Osama bin Laden was never formally indicted; he was ultimately killed by [U.S. special operations forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DEVGRU) on May 2, 2011, in [his compound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden's_compound_in_Abbottabad) in [Abbottabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbottabad), Pakistan, after a [10-year manhunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhunt_for_Osama_bin_Laden).[^cite_note-420] The [main trial of the attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_v._Khalid_Sheikh_Mohammed) against Mohammed and his co-conspirators Walid bin Attash, Ramzi bin al-Shibh, [Ammar al-Baluchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ammar_al-Baluchi), and [Mustafa Ahmad al-Hawsawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Ahmad_al-Hawsawi) remains unresolved. Khalid Sheikh Mohammed was arrested on March 1, 2003, in [Rawalpindi](343524.md), Pakistan, by Pakistani security officials working with the CIA. He was then held at multiple [CIA secret prisons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_site) and Guantanamo Bay detention camp, where he was interrogated and tortured with methods including [waterboarding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterboarding).[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422]

In 2003, al-Hawsawi and Abd al-Aziz Ali were arrested and transferred to U.S. custody. Both would later be accused of providing money and travel assistance to the hijackers.[^cite_note-423] During U.S. hearings at Guantanamo Bay in March 2007, Mohammed again confessed his responsibility for the attacks, stating he "was responsible for the 9/11 operation from A to Z" and that his statement was not made under duress.[^cite_note-aljazeera2007-63][^cite_note-424] In January 2023, the U.S. government opened up about a potential [plea deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plea_deal),[^cite_note-\:1-425] with Biden giving up on the effort in September that year.[^cite_note-426]

To date, only peripheral persons have thus been convicted for charges in connection with the attacks. These include:

- [Zacarias Moussaoui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zacarias_Moussaoui) who was indicted in December 2001 and sentenced to life in prison without the possibility of parole in May 2006 by a U.S. federal jury

- [Mounir el-Motassadeq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mounir_el-Motassadeq) who was first convicted in February 2003 by a [Federal Court of Justice](561684.md) in Germany and was deported to [Morocco](19291.md) in October 2018 after serving his sentence

  [^cite_note-427]

- [Abu Dahdah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dahdah) who was arrested in November 2001, sentenced by a [Spanish High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_High_Court) and released from prison in May 2013.

  [^cite_note-428]

In July 2024, *[The New York Times](30680.md)* reported that Mohammed, bin Attash, and al-Hawsawi had agreed to plead guilty to conspiracy in exchange for life sentences, avoiding trial and execution. However, U.S. Defense Secretary [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) revoked a plea agreement with Mohammed days later.[^cite_note-429]

## Investigations

### FBI

Further information: [Hijackers in the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hijackers_in_the_September_11_attacks)

Immediately after the attacks, the [Federal Bureau of Investigation](11127.md) (FBI) started [PENTTBOM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PENTTBOM), the largest criminal inquiry in U.S. history. At its height, more than half of the FBI's agents worked on the investigation and followed a half-million leads.[^cite_note-Pentbomb-430] The FBI concluded that there was "clear and irrefutable" evidence linking al-Qaeda and bin Laden to the attacks.[^cite_note-431]



[Mohammed Atta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Atta)

The FBI quickly identified the hijackers, including leader Atta, when his luggage was discovered at Boston's Logan Airport. Atta had been forced to check two of his three bags due to space limitations on the 19-seat commuter flight he took to Boston. Due to a new policy instituted to prevent flight delays, the luggage failed to make it aboard American Airlines Flight 11 as planned. The luggage contained the hijackers' names, assignments, and al-Qaeda connections. "It had all these Arab-language \[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*] papers that amounted to the [Rosetta stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosetta_stone) of the investigation," said one FBI agent.[^cite_note-Unraveling_9-11_Was_in_the_Bags-432]

Within hours of the attacks, the FBI released the names and in many cases the personal details of the suspected pilots and hijackers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarke200413–14-433][^cite_note-FBI_19-434] Abu Jandal, who served as bin Laden's chief bodyguard for years, confirmed the identity of seven hijackers as al-Qaeda members during interrogations with the FBI on September 17. He had been jailed in a Yemeni prison since 2000.[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436] On September 27, photos of all 19 hijackers were released, along with information about possible nationalities and aliases.[^cite_note-437] Fifteen of the men were from Saudi Arabia, two were from the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md), one was from Egypt, and one was from Lebanon.[^cite_note-438]



[Ziad Jarrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ziad_Jarrah)

By midday, the U.S. National Security Agency and German intelligence agencies had intercepted communications pointing to Osama bin Laden.[^cite_note-439] Two of the hijackers were known to have traveled with a bin Laden associate to Malaysia in 2000[^cite_note-440] and Atta had previously gone to Afghanistan.[^cite_note-441] He and others were part of a terrorist cell in Hamburg, Germany.[^cite_note-442] One of the members of the Hamburg cell in Germany was discovered to have been in communication with Khalid Sheikh Mohammed who was identified as a member of al-Qaeda.[^cite_note-443]

Authorities in the United States and the United Kingdom also obtained electronic intercepts, including telephone conversations and electronic bank transfers, which indicated that Mohammed Atef was a key figure in the planning of the 9/11 attacks. Intercepts were also obtained of conversations that took place days before September 11 between bin Laden and an associate in Pakistan referring to "an incident that would take place in America on, or around, September 11" and discussing potential repercussions. In another conversation with an associate in Afghanistan, bin Laden discussed the "scale and effects of a forthcoming operation." These conversations did not specifically mention the World Trade Center, the Pentagon, or other specifics.[^cite_note-444]

| Nationality | Number |
| --- | --- |
| [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) | 15 |
| [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) | 2 |
| [Egypt](8087628.md) | 1 |
| [Lebanon](17771.md) | 1 |

In their annual violent crime index for the year 2001, the FBI recorded the deaths from the attacks as murder, in separate tables so as not to mix them with other reported crimes for that year.[^cite_note-445] In a disclaimer, the FBI stated that "the number of deaths is so great that combining it with the traditional [crime statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_statistics) will have an [outlier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outlier) effect that falsely skews all types of measurements in the program's analyses."[^cite_note-446] New York City also did not include the deaths in their annual crime statistics for 2001.[^cite_note-HuffPost_story-447]

### CIA

Further information: [September 11 intelligence before the attacks](35176335.md)

In 2004, [John L. Helgerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_L._Helgerson), the Inspector General of the [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) (CIA), conducted an internal review of the agency's pre-9/11 performance and was harshly critical of senior CIA officials for not doing everything possible to confront terrorism.[^cite_note-Spiegel_2009-448] According to [Philip Giraldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_Giraldi) in *[The American Conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_American_Conservative)*, Helgerson criticized their failure to stop two of the 9/11 hijackers, Nawaf al-Hazmi and Khalid al-Mihdhar, as they entered the United States and their failure to share information on the two men with the FBI.[^cite_note-449]

In May 2007, senators from both major U.S. political parties (the [Republican](32070.md) and [Democratic](5043544.md) parties) drafted legislation to make the review public. One of the backers, Senator [Ron Wyden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Wyden) said, "The American people have a right to know what the Central Intelligence Agency was doing in those critical months before 9/11."[^cite_note-450] The report was released in 2009 by President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama).[^cite_note-Spiegel_2009-448]

### Congressional inquiry

Main article: [Joint Inquiry into Intelligence Community Activities before and after the Terrorist Attacks of September 11, 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Inquiry_into_Intelligence_Community_Activities_before_and_after_the_Terrorist_Attacks_of_September_11,_2001)

In February 2002, the [Senate Select Committee on Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Select_Committee_on_Intelligence) and the [House Permanent Select Committee on Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Permanent_Select_Committee_on_Intelligence) formed a joint inquiry into the performance of the [U.S. Intelligence Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Intelligence_Community).[^cite_note-451] Their 832-page report released in December 2002[^cite_note-452] detailed failings of the FBI and CIA to use available information, including about terrorists the CIA knew were in the United States, to disrupt the plots.[^cite_note-Theoharis_2006_p222–224-453] The joint inquiry developed its information about possible involvement of Saudi Arabian government officials from non-classified sources.[^cite_note-McClatchy_DC_2013-08-12-454] The Bush administration demanded 28 related pages remain classified.[^cite_note-Theoharis_2006_p222–224-453]

In December 2002, the inquiry's chair [Bob Graham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Graham) revealed in an interview that there was "evidence that there were foreign governments involved in facilitating the activities of at least some of the terrorists in the United States."[^cite_note-455] Victim families were frustrated by the unanswered questions and redacted material from the congressional inquiry and demanded an independent commission.[^cite_note-Theoharis_2006_p222–224-453] September 11 victim families,[^cite_note-456] members of Congress[^cite_note-457] and the Saudi Arabian government are still seeking the release of the documents.[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459] In June 2016, CIA chief [John Brennan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_O._Brennan) said that he believes 28 redacted pages of a congressional inquiry into 9/11 will soon be made public, and that they will prove that the government of Saudi Arabia had no involvement in the September 11 attacks.[^cite_note-460]

In September 2016, Congress passed the [Justice Against Sponsors of Terrorism Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_Against_Sponsors_of_Terrorism_Act) that would allow relatives of victims of the September 11 attacks to sue [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) for its government's [alleged role in the attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alleged_Saudi_government_role_in_the_September_11_attacks).[^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462][^cite_note-463]

### 9/11 Commission

See also: [Criticism of the 9/11 Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_the_9/11_Commission)

The National Commission on Terrorist Attacks Upon the United States, popularly known as the [9/11 Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Commission), chaired by [Thomas Kean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Kean),[^cite_note-468] was formed in late 2002 to prepare a thorough account of the circumstances surrounding the attacks, including preparedness for and the immediate response to the attacks.[^cite_note-469] The commission issued the *[9/11 Commission Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Commission_Report)* in July 2004, a 585-page report based on its investigations. The report detailed the events leading up to the attacks, concluding that they were carried out by al-Qaeda.[^cite_note-470] The commission also examined how security and intelligence agencies were inadequately coordinated to prevent the attacks.[^cite_note-471]

According to the report, "We believe the 9/11 attacks revealed four kinds of failures: in imagination, policy, capabilities, and management."[^cite_note-472] The commission made numerous recommendations on how to prevent future attacks, and in 2011 was dismayed that several of its recommendations had yet to be implemented.[^cite_note-473]

### National Institute of Standards and Technology

Main article: [NIST World Trade Center Disaster Investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NIST_World_Trade_Center_Disaster_Investigation)

See also: [7 World Trade Center (1987–2001) § 9/11 and collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7_World_Trade_Center_\(1987%E2%80%932001\)#9/11_and_collapse)



The exterior support columns from the South Tower remained standing after the building collapsed

The U.S. National Institute of Standards and Technology investigated the collapses of the Twin Towers and 7 WTC. The investigations examined why the buildings collapsed and what fire protection measures were in place, and evaluated how fire protection systems might be improved in future construction.[^cite_note-NISTInvest-474] The investigation into the collapse of 1 WTC and 2 WTC was concluded in October 2005 and that of 7 WTC was completed in August 2008.[^cite_note-NIST_WTC_7_Investigation-475]

NIST found that the fireproofing on the Twin Towers' steel infrastructures was blown off by the initial impact of the planes and that had this not occurred, the towers likely would have remained standing.[^cite_note-NISTCollapse-476] A 2007 study of the north tower's collapse published by researchers at [Purdue University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purdue_University) determined that since the plane's impact had stripped off much of the structure's thermal insulation, the heat from a typical office fire would have softened and weakened the exposed girders and columns enough to initiate the collapse regardless of the number of columns cut or damaged by the impact.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478]

The director of the original investigation stated that "the towers did amazingly well. The terrorist aircraft didn't bring the buildings down; it was the fire that followed. It was proven that you could take out two-thirds of the columns in a tower and the building would still stand."[^cite_note-TerrorProof-479] The fires weakened the trusses supporting the floors, making the floors sag. The sagging floors pulled on the exterior steel columns causing the exterior columns to bow inward.

With the damage to the core columns, the buckling exterior columns could no longer support the buildings, causing them to collapse. Additionally, the report found the towers' stairwells were not properly reinforced to provide adequate [emergency escape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_escape) for people above the impact zones.[^cite_note-NIST-480] NIST concluded that uncontrolled fires in 7 WTC caused floor beams and girders to heat and subsequently "caused a critical support column to fail, initiating a fire-induced progressive collapse that brought the building down."[^cite_note-NIST_WTC_7_Investigation-475]

### Alleged Saudi government role

Main article: [Alleged Saudi government role in the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alleged_Saudi_government_role_in_the_September_11_attacks)

See also: [Saudi Arabia–United States relations](9753135.md), [Saudi Arabia and state-sponsored terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_and_state-sponsored_terrorism), and [The 28 pages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_28_pages)

The Obama administration released a document compiled by U.S. investigators Dana Lesemann and Michael Jacobson, known as "File 17,"[^cite_note-481] which contains a list naming three dozen people, including the suspected [Saudi intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_intelligence) officers attached to Saudi Arabia's embassy in Washington, D.C.,[^cite_note-482] which connects Saudi Arabia to the hijackers.[^cite_note-483][^cite_note-484]

Congress passed the [Justice Against Sponsors of Terrorism Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_Against_Sponsors_of_Terrorism_Act).[^cite_note-Stempel-485][^cite_note-486] The practical effect of the legislation was to allow the continuation of a longstanding civil lawsuit brought by families of victims of the September 11 attacks against Saudi Arabia for its government's alleged role in the attacks.[^cite_note-487] A U.S. judge formally allowed a suit to move forward against the government of Saudi Arabia brought by 9/11 survivors and victims' families.[^cite_note-Stempel-485]

The families of some 9/11 victims obtained two videos and a notepad seized from Saudi national [Omar al-Bayoumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omar_al-Bayoumi) by the British courts. The first video showed him hosting a party in [San Diego](28504.md) for Nawaf al-Hazmi and Khalid al-Mihdhar, the first two hijackers to arrive in the U.S. The other video showed al-Bayoumi greeting the cleric [Anwar al-Awlaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_al-Awlaki), who was blamed for radicalizing Americans and later killed in a CIA drone strike. The notepad depicted a hand-drawn airplane and some mathematical equations that, according to a pilot's court statement, might have been used to calculate the rate of descent to get to a target. According to a 2017 FBI memo, from the late 1990s until the 9/11 attack, al-Bayoumi was a paid [cooptee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooptee) of the Saudi [General Intelligence Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Intelligence_Presidency). As of April 2022 he is believed to be living in Saudi Arabia, which has denied any involvement in 9/11.[^cite_note-488]

## Rebuilding and memorials

### Reconstruction

Main articles: [Rescue and recovery effort after the September 11 attacks on the World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rescue_and_recovery_effort_after_the_September_11_attacks_on_the_World_Trade_Center) and [Construction of One World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construction_of_One_World_Trade_Center)

Further information: [World Trade Center (2001–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(2001%E2%80%93present\)) and [World Trade Center site](67173.md)



The [rebuilt World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(2001%E2%80%93present\)), September 2020

Within hours of the attack, a search and rescue operation was launched. After months of around-the-clock operations, the World Trade Center site was cleared by the end of May 2002.[^cite_note-489] The damaged section of the Pentagon was rebuilt and occupied within a year of the attacks.[^cite_note-490] The temporary [World Trade Center PATH station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_PATH_station) opened in late 2003 and construction of the new 7 World Trade Center was completed in 2006. Work on rebuilding the main World Trade Center site was delayed until late 2006 when leaseholder [Larry Silverstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Silverstein) and the Port Authority of New York and New Jersey agreed on financing.[^cite_note-491] The construction of [One World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_World_Trade_Center) began in April 2006, and reached its full height in May 2013. The spire was installed atop the building at that date, putting One WTC's height at 1,776 feet (541 m), making it the tallest building in the [Western Hemisphere](73162.md).[^cite_note-492][^cite_note-Moore_One_WTC-493] One WTC finished construction and opened on November 3, 2014.[^cite_note-Moore_One_WTC-493][^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495]

On the World Trade Center site, three more office towers were to be built one block east of where the original towers stood.[^cite_note-496] [4 WTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4_World_Trade_Center), meanwhile, opened in November 2013, making it the second tower on the site to open behind 7 World Trade Center, as well as the first building on the Port Authority property.[^cite_note-497] 3 WTC opened in June 2018, becoming the fourth skyscraper at the site to be completed.[^cite_note-USAToday_2018-06-10-498] In December 2022, the Nicholas Greek Orthodox Church fully reopened for regular services,[^cite_note-CBS_News_20222-499] followed by the opening of the [Ronald O. Perelman Performing Arts Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_O._Perelman_Performing_Arts_Center) in September 2023.[^cite_note-500] With construction beginning in 2008,[^cite_note-501] [2 World Trade Center](41256500.md) remains as of 2026 unfinished.[^cite_note-502] Scale models of the building were publicly revealed in September 2024, although [Silverstein Properties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverstein_Properties) was still trying to secure funding for the tower at the time.[^cite_note-503][^cite_note-504]

### Memorials

Main article: [Memorials and services for the September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorials_and_services_for_the_September_11_attacks)



The [National September 11 Memorial and Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_September_11_Memorial_and_Museum) in [Lower Manhattan](455646.md), August 2016

In the days following the attacks, many memorials and vigils were held around the world, and photographs of the dead and missing were posted around [Ground Zero](67173.md). A witness described being unable to "get away from faces of innocent victims who were killed. Their pictures are everywhere, on phone booths, street lights, and walls of subway stations. Everything reminded me of a huge funeral, people were quiet and sad, but also very nice. Before, New York gave me a cold feeling; now people were reaching out to help each other."[^cite_note-505] President Bush proclaimed Friday, September 14, 2001, as Patriot Day.[^cite_note-506]



*[Tribute in Light](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribute_in_Light)*, featuring two columns of light representing the Twin Towers, September 2020

One of the first memorials was the *[Tribute in Light](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribute_in_Light)*, an installation of 88 searchlights at the footprints of the World Trade Center towers.[^cite_note-507] In New York City, the [World Trade Center Site Memorial Competition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_Site_Memorial_Competition) was held to design an appropriate memorial on the site.[^cite_note-508] The winning design, *[Reflecting Absence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reflecting_Absence)*, was selected in August 2006, and consists of a pair of reflecting pools in the footprints of the towers, surrounded by a list of the victims' names in an underground memorial space.[^cite_note-509] The memorial was completed on the 10th anniversary of the attacks in 2011;[^cite_note-510] a museum also opened on site in May 2014.[^cite_note-511]

*[The Sphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sphere)* by the German sculptor [Fritz Koenig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fritz_Koenig) is the world's largest bronze sculpture of modern times, and stood between the Twin Towers on the [Austin J. Tobin Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_J._Tobin_Plaza) from 1971 until the attacks. The sculpture, weighing more than 20 tons, was the only remaining work of art to be recovered largely intact from the ruins of the towers. Since then, the work of art, known in the U.S. as *The Sphere*, has been transformed into a symbolic monument of 9/11 commemoration. After being dismantled and stored near a hangar at [John F. Kennedy International Airport](97218.md), the sculpture was the subject of the 2001 documentary *[The Sphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sphere#Documentary)* by filmmaker [Percy Adlon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Percy_Adlon). In August 2017, the work was installed at [Liberty Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_Park), close to the new World Trade Center aerial and the [9/11 Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Memorial).[^cite_note-NYT_2017-11-29-512]



The [National 9/11 Pentagon Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_9/11_Pentagon_Memorial) in [Arlington County, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia), September 2008

In Arlington County, the [Pentagon Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentagon_Memorial) was completed and opened to the public on the seventh anniversary of the attacks in 2008.[^cite_note-513][^cite_note-514] It consists of a landscaped park with 184 benches facing the Pentagon.[^cite_note-dwyer-may2007-515] When the Pentagon was repaired in 2001–2002, a private chapel and indoor memorial were included at the area where Flight 77 crashed into the building.[^cite_note-516]

In Shanksville, a [concrete-and-glass visitor center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flight_93_National_Memorial) was opened in 2015,[^cite_note-NPS_93_mem-517] situated on a hill overlooking the crash site and the white marble *Wall of Names*.[^cite_note-NPS_FAQs-518] An observation platform at the visitor center and the white marble wall are both aligned beneath the path of Flight 93.[^cite_note-NPS_FAQs-518][^cite_note-NYT_2015-09-09-519] New York City firefighters donated a cross made of steel from the World Trade Center and mounted on top of a platform shaped like the Pentagon.[^cite_note-ap2008-08-24-520] It was installed outside the firehouse on August 25, 2008.[^cite_note-521] Many other permanent memorials are elsewhere. Scholarships and charities have been established by the victims' families and by many other organizations and private figures.[^cite_note-522]

On every anniversary in New York City, the names of the victims who died there are read out over music. The President of the United States attends a memorial service at the Pentagon,[^cite_note-523] and asks Americans to observe [Patriot Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriot_Day) with a moment of silence. Smaller services are held in Shanksville, Pennsylvania, which are usually attended by the First Lady. In 2023, Joe Biden did not attend services in the affected areas, instead marking the day in [Anchorage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage), Alaska, the only U.S. president to do so since the attacks.[^cite_note-524][^cite_note-525][^cite_note-526]

## See also

[^cite_note-8]: &#x20;Sources vary regarding the number of injuries―some say 6,000<sup>[\[5\]](#cite_note-6)</sup> while others go as high as 25,000.<sup>[\[6\]](#cite_note-7)</sup>

[^cite_note-12]: &#x20;[Al-Qaeda](1921.md)'s name for the events is the **Manhattan Raid**;<sup>[\[7\]](#cite_note-9)</sup> an example, perhaps the earliest known or knowable example, is found in a September 2006–produced video.<sup>[\[8\]](#cite_note-ManhattanRaid-10)</sup><sup>[\[9\]](#cite_note-11)</sup>

[^cite_note-14]: &#x20;Pronounced "nine eleven"<sup>[\[10\]](#cite_note-13)</sup>

[^cite_note-Time-20]: &#x20;The exact time is disputed. The *9/11 Commission Report* states that Flight 175 struck the South Tower at 09\:03\:11 a.m.,<sup>[\[11\]](#cite_note-15)</sup><sup>[\[12\]](#cite_note-16)</sup> NIST reports 09\:02\:59 a.m.,<sup>[\[13\]](#cite_note-17)</sup> and some other sources claim 09\:03\:02 a.m.<sup>[\[14\]](#cite_note-18)</sup> In any case, the 16-minute gap between each impact is rounded to 17.<sup>[\[15\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTE9/11_Commission2004a302-19)</sup>

[^cite_note-Time1-25]: &#x20;While NIST and the 9/11 Commission give differing accounts of the exact second of the North Tower's collapse initiation, with NIST placing it at 10\:28\:22 a.m.<sup>[\[16\]](#cite_note-21)</sup><sup>[\[17\]](#cite_note-22)</sup> and the commission at 10\:28\:25 a.m.,<sup>[\[18\]](#cite_note-23)</sup> it is generally accepted that Flight 11 did not strike the North Tower any sooner than 8\:46\:26 a.m.,<sup>[\[19\]](#cite_note-102mins-24)</sup> so the time it took for the North Tower to start to collapse was just shy of 102 minutes either way.

[^cite_note-26]: &#x20;Excluding the hijackers

[^cite_note-EDT-109]: &#x20;[Eastern Daylight Time](496028.md) (UTC−04\:00)

[^cite_note-110]: &#x20;Excluding hijackers

[^cite_note-111]: &#x20;Including emergency workers

[^cite_note-112]: &#x20;Including hijackers

[^cite_note-113]: &#x20;The aircraft was a Boeing 767-200(Extended Range "ER") model; Boeing [assigns a unique code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Boeing_customer_codes) for each company that buys one of its aircraft, which is applied as an [infix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infix) to the model number at the time the aircraft is built, hence "767-223(ER)" designates a 767-200 built for American Airlines (customer code 23).

[^cite_note-114]: &#x20;The aircraft was a Boeing 767-200 model; Boeing [assigns a unique code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Boeing_customer_codes) for each company that buys one of its aircraft, which is applied as an [infix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infix) to the model number at the time the aircraft is built, hence "767-222" designates a 767-200 built for United Airlines (customer code 22).

[^cite_note-115]: &#x20;The aircraft was a Boeing 757-200 model; Boeing [assigns a unique code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Boeing_customer_codes) for each company that buys one of its aircraft, which is applied as an [infix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infix) to the model number at the time the aircraft is built, hence "757-223" designates a 757-200 built for American Airlines (customer code 23).

[^cite_note-116]: &#x20;The aircraft was a Boeing 757-200 model; Boeing [assigns a unique code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Boeing_customer_codes) for each company that buys one of its aircraft, which is applied as an [infix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infix) to the model number at the time the aircraft is built, hence "757-222" designates a 757-200 built for United Airlines (customer code 22).

[^cite_note-150]: &#x20;NIST and the 9/11 Commission both state that the collapse began at 9\:58\:59 a.m., which is rounded to 9\:59<sup>[\[133\]](#cite_note-auto-148)</sup><sup>: 84 </sup><sup>[\[134\]](#cite_note-finalreport-149)</sup><sup>: 322 </sup> for simplicity. If the commission's claim that the South Tower was struck at 9\:03\:11 is to be believed, then the collapse began 55 minutes and 48 seconds after the crash, not 56 minutes.

[^cite_note-153]: &#x20;The exact time of the North Tower's collapse initiation is disputed, with NIST dubbing the moment it began to collapse as being 10\:28\:22 a.m.<sup>[\[135\]](#cite_note-151)</sup> and the 9/11 Commission recording the time as 10\:28\:25.<sup>[\[136\]](#cite_note-DOD_Cronk_Biden_Announces-152)</sup><sup>: 329 </sup>

[^cite_note-169]: &#x20;The [massacre at Camp Speicher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_Speicher_massacre)―often described as the second deadliest act of terrorism in history after 9/11―is said to have killed between 1,095 and 1,700 people.<sup>[\[151\]](#cite_note-168)</sup> The upper estimate would tie it with the attack on the World Trade Center's North Tower, but until the true death toll of the massacre becomes known, then the hijacking and crash of Flight 11 was the deadliest act of terrorism on record.

[^cite_note-420]: &#x20;President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) announced his death on May 1. At the time of the raid, it was early morning of May 2 in Pakistan and late afternoon of May 1 in the U.S.<sup>[\[401\]](#cite_note-419)</sup>

[^cite_note-468]: &#x20;Former Secretary of State [Henry Kissinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Kissinger) was initially appointed to head the commission<sup>[\[445\]](#cite_note-464)</sup> but resigned only weeks after being appointed, to avoid conflicts of interest.<sup>[\[446\]](#cite_note-465)</sup> Former U.S. Senator [George Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_J._Mitchell) was originally appointed as the vice chairman, but he stepped down on December 10, 2002, not wanting to sever ties to his law firm.<sup>[\[447\]](#cite_note-466)</sup> On December 15, 2002, Bush appointed former New Jersey governor [Thomas Kean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Kean) to head the commission.<sup>[\[448\]](#cite_note-467)</sup>

Article 5: The Parties agree that an armed attack against one or more of them in Europe or North America shall be considered an attack against them all and consequently they agree that, if such an armed attack occurs, each of them, in the exercise of the right of individual or collective self-defence recognised by Article 51 of the Charter of the United Nations, will assist the Party or Parties so attacked by taking forthwith, individually and in concert with the other Parties, such action as it deems necessary, including the use of armed force, to restore and maintain the security of the North Atlantic area. Any such armed attack and all measures taken as a result thereof shall immediately be reported to the Security Council. Such measures shall be terminated when the Security Council has taken the measures necessary to restore and maintain international peace and security.

As the aircraft debris went through several stories in the tower, much of the thermal insulation on the core columns would have been scoured off. Under such conditions, the ensuing fire would be sufficient to cause instability and initiate collapse. From an engineering perspective, impact damage to the core structure had a negligible effect on the critical thermal load required to initiate collapse in the core structure.

[^cite_note-478]: &#x20;Tally, Steve (June 12, 2007). ["Purdue creates scientifically based animation of 9/11 attack"](https://web.archive.org/web/20181231084252/https://news.uns.purdue.edu/x/2007a/070612HoffmannWTC.html). *Purdue News Service*. Archived from [the original](http://news.uns.purdue.edu/x/2007a/070612HoffmannWTC.html) on December 31, 2018. Retrieved September 4, 2011. The aircraft moved through the building as if it were a hot and fast lava flow", Sozen says. "Consequently, much of the fireproofing insulation was ripped off the structure. Even if all of the columns and girders had survived the impact – an unlikely event – the structure would fail as the result of a buckling of the columns. The heat from an ordinary office fire would suffice to soften and weaken the unprotected steel. Evaluation of the effects of the fire on the core column structure, with the insulation removed by the impact, showed that collapse would follow whatever the number of columns cut at the time of the impact.
